{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We derive the nonlinear Boltzmann equation from a grand-canonical Newtonian gas with initial pair exclusion. We treat stable, finite-range radial potentials that are <i>C</i><sup>2</sup> except for an allowed repulsive singularity at the origin, and <i>C</i><sup>1</sup> initial probability densities with spatially summable Gaussian bounds on the density and its spatial gradient. All fixed-order rescaled factorial marginals converge in <i>L</i><sup>1</sup>, uniformly throughout every finite interval on which the classical kinetic solution has a uniform Gaussian bound. The result allows attractive wells and dynamically formed clusters, without restrictions on the differential scattering cross-section.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We derive the nonlinear Boltzmann equation from a grand-canonical Newtonian gas with initial pair exclusion. We treat stable, finite-range radial potentials that are <i>C</i><sup>2</sup> except for an allowed repulsive singularity at the origin, and <i>C</i><sup>1</sup> initial probability densities with spatially summable Gaussian bounds on the density and its spatial gradient. All fixed-order rescaled factorial marginals converge in <i>L</i><sup>1</sup>, uniformly throughout every finite interval on which the classical kinetic solution has a uniform Gaussian bound. The result allows attractive wells and dynamically formed clusters, without restrictions on the differential scattering cross-section.

- **claim-main-chaos** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $\Phi$ satisfy Assumption <a href="#pre:potential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:potential">1</a>, and let $\beta>0$. Let $f_0\ge0$ be a $C^1$ probability density on $\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3$ such that $$\label{intro:data-bound} \\|f_0\\|_{\mathrm{Bol},2\beta} +\\|\nabla_xf_0\\|_{\mathrm{Bol},2\beta}<\infty.$$ Fix $0<T<\infty$ for which the equation $$\label{intro:boltzmann} (\partial_t+v\cdot\nabla_x)f=Q_\Phi(f,f), \qquad f\|_{t=0}=f_0,$$ has a nonnegative classical probability-density solution satisfying $$\label{intro:solution-bound} \sup_{0\le t\le T}\sup_{x,v} e^{2\beta\|v\|^2}f(t,x,v)<\infty.$$ Start the exact Newtonian process from the grand-canonical law <a href="#pre:model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:model">[pre:model]</a>. For its scaled factorial densities $F_s^\varepsilon$, defined in <a href="#pre:factorial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:factorial">[pre:factorial]</a>, every fixed integer $s\ge1$ satisfies $$\label{intro:chaos} \lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}\; \sup_{0\le t\le T} \bigl\\|F_s^\varepsilon(t)-f(t)^{\otimes s}\bigr\\|_ {L^1((\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3)^s)}=0.$$
  - Explanation: For a stable finite-range radial potential and regular Gaussian-bounded data, every fixed-order rescaled factorial density converges in L1 to the tensor power of the kinetic solution, uniformly on the whole kinetic interval.
- **claim-empirical-observables** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-empirical)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, let $a\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3;\mathbb R)$ and $\eta>0$. Then $$\label{intro:empirical} \lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}\; \sup_{0\le t\le T} \mathbb P\!\left( \left\|\mu_\varepsilon^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}a(z_i(t)) -\int a(z)f(t,z)\,dz\right\|>\eta\right)=0.$$
  - Explanation: Smooth compactly supported empirical observables converge in probability to their kinetic integral at deterministic times, uniformly over the interval.
- **claim-global-flow** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-flow)): For each fixed $\varepsilon>0$ and finite label set $R$, <a href="#pre:dynamics" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:dynamics">[pre:dynamics]</a> has a unique global flow from every finite-energy initial state in its domain. The flow preserves phase volume and $\mathcal H_R$. In the singular case, every pair distance along a given trajectory is bounded away from zero. In the nonsingular case coincident positions are permitted.
  - Explanation: The finite-particle Newtonian dynamics has a unique global flow that preserves phase volume and energy; in the singular case pair distances stay positive along trajectories.
- **claim-initial-centering** (bound, [source](#stmt-pre-initial)): Put $\rho_0(x)=\int f_0(x,v)\,dv$, and assume $\\|\rho_0\\|_\infty<\infty$. At time zero, $$\label{eq:pre-insertion} \begin{split} \widetilde F_R^\varepsilon(0,Z_R) &=F_R^\varepsilon(0,Z_R)\\ &=f_0^{\otimes R}(Z_R)\mathbf1_{{\cal D}_R^\varepsilon}(Z_R) \mathbb E\prod_{i\in R}\mathbf1_{\{N(B_i)=0\}}, \qquad B_i=\overline B(x_i,\varepsilon). \end{split}$$ The expectation is over an independent cloud with the original law, and $N(B)$ counts its positions in $B$. Consequently $0\leq F_R^\varepsilon(0)\leq f_0^{\otimes R}$.* *For $Z_R\in{\cal D}_R^\varepsilon$, define $$\label{eq:pre-initialcenter} E_R^\varepsilon(0,Z_R)= \sum_{S\subset R}(-1)^{\|R\setminus S\|} f_0^{\otimes(R\setminus S)}(Z_{R\setminus S}) \widetilde F_S^\varepsilon(0,Z_S).$$ There are $c_0>0,C_0<\infty$, independent of $R,\varepsilon$, such that for sufficiently small $\varepsilon$, $$\label{eq:pre-centersmall} \|E_R^\varepsilon(0,Z_R)\| \leq(C_0\varepsilon^{c_0})^{\|R\|}f_0^{\otimes R}(Z_R).$$ One may take $c_0=1/125$. For fixed $r$, $$\label{eq:pre-mass} 0\leq1-\int F_R^\varepsilon(0,Z_R)\,dZ_R \leq Cr\varepsilon+Cr^2\varepsilon^3, \qquad \int F_R^\varepsilon(t)=\int F_R^\varepsilon(0).$$
  - Explanation: At time zero the externally isolated and plain factorial densities coincide, centered correlations are small per label, and the mass defect is controlled.
- **claim-residence-flux** (bound, [source](#stmt-pre-duration)): For every $M<\infty$ there is $C_M<\infty$ such that $$\label{eq:pre-duration} \int_{\substack{\|g\|\leq M\\n\in S^2,\ n\cdot g<0}} \tau_*(n,g)\|n\cdot g\|\,d\sigma(n)\,dg\leq C_M.$$ The incoming flux of $\{\tau_*>D,\ \|g\|\leq M\}$ is at most $C_M/D$. In particular $\tau_*<\infty$ almost everywhere in incoming flux. The entrance-to-exit scattering map preserves flux.
  - Explanation: The residence time inside the interaction ball is integrable against incoming flux, so infinite durations have zero flux and scattering preserves flux.
- **claim-collision-reversibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-operator)): The measure $d\Lambda=\|v-v_*\|\,dv\,dv_*\,d^2b$ is preserved by exchanging incoming and outgoing velocity pairs with the corresponding entrance parameter. The weak definition <a href="#pre:collision" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:collision">[pre:collision]</a> equals $$\label{eq:pre-Qstrong} Q_\Phi(f,f)(x,v)= \int_{\mathbb R^3}\|v-v_*\| \int_{\substack{b\in(v-v_*)^\perp\\\|b\|<1}} [\,f(x,v')f(x,v_*')-f(x,v)f(x,v_*)\,]\,d^2b\,dv_* .$$ These identities hold whenever the integrals are absolutely convergent, in particular for Gaussian bounded functions. All scattering branches are included, and the impact disk has area $\pi$.
  - Explanation: The collision measure is invariant under the scattering involution and exchange, which turns the weak collision operator into its strong gain-minus-loss form without a differential cross-section.
- **claim-scattering-nondegeneracy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-scattering)): Outside a set of zero incoming flux, an encounter either is a straight force-free passage or satisfies $$\label{eq:pre-increments} v'\ne v,\qquad v_*'\ne v .$$ For $M<\infty$ and $\eta>0$, the non-free relative incoming data $(n,g)$ with $\|g\|\leq M$ can be restricted to a rotation-invariant compact set, losing at most $\eta$ relative incoming flux, on which the duration is bounded and $\|g\|$, $\|v'-v\|$, and $\|v_*'-v\|$ have positive lower bounds. For fixed radial scattering parameters, each of these nonzero increments has a uniform spherical directional marginal under common rotation. The two increments need not be independent.
  - Explanation: Outside a null set of incoming flux an encounter is a free passage or produces nonzero velocity increments, with compact rotation-invariant restrictions and uniform directional marginals.
- **claim-kinetic-envelopes** (bound, [source](#stmt-pre-envelopes)): Suppose $f$ is the classical nonnegative solution in the statement, and $$A=\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\\|f(t)\\|_{\infty,2\beta}<\infty,\qquad B_0=\\|f_0\\|_{\mathrm{Bol},2\beta} +\\|\nabla_xf_0\\|_{\mathrm{Bol},2\beta}<\infty.$$ Then $$\label{eq:pre-envelope} \sup_{0\leq t\leq T} \left(\\|f(t)\\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\beta} +\\|\nabla_xf(t)\\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\beta}\right) \leq C(A,B_0,\beta,T)<\infty.$$ Fix, once and for all, $$\label{eq:pre-beta0} \beta_0=\beta/2.$$ Thus all independent inputs $f(t)$ and their spatial Lipschitz envelopes have a common bound with the fixed margin $\beta-\beta_0$. Constants in <a href="#eq:pre-envelope" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre-envelope">[eq:pre-envelope]</a> are independent of the subsequent time-layer subdivision.
  - Explanation: The assumed classical kinetic solution and its spatial gradient inherit a summed spatial Gaussian bound at a reduced exponent, with constants independent of the later layer subdivision.
- **claim-cell-expansion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cell-expansion)): Let $R\subset S$ and let $A_0$ be the union of the blocks of $\pi_0(S)$ meeting $R$. For $R\ne\varnothing$, let $\mathcal P$ be the collection of the other blocks of $\pi_0(S)$. Then $$\mathsf H^I_{R,S} =\sum_{\mathcal U\subset\mathcal P} \mathsf K^I_{R,A_0\cup\bigcup\mathcal U}, \label{eq:cell-subset-sum}$$ and, as an equality of signed measures, $$\mathsf K^I_{R,A_0\cup\bigcup\mathcal U} =\sum_{\mathcal V\subset\mathcal U} (-1)^{\|\mathcal U\|-\|\mathcal V\|} \mathsf H^I_{R,A_0\cup\bigcup\mathcal V}. \label{eq:cell-boolean}$$ Every coefficient is evaluated on its displayed input coordinates. If $m=\|S\setminus R\|$, the number of diagrams in <a href="#eq:cell-K" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cell-K">[eq:cell-K]</a> is at most $(C(1+m))^{Cm}$, with a universal $C$. There is no partition of the roots in this count.
  - Explanation: The endpoint-isolated transport kernel on one cell is an exact finite sum over whole-polymer subsets, inverted by Boolean inclusion-exclusion, with a counted number of diagrams.
- **claim-factorial-normalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-cell-factorial)): For the actual microscopic cloud and $r=\|R\|$, the cell identity is written after relabelling the ordered roots as $R=\{1,\ldots,r\}$: $$\widetilde F_R(b) =\sum_{m\geq0}\frac{\mu^m}{m!} \mathcal T^I_{R,R\sqcup\{r+1,\ldots,r+m\}} \widetilde F_{r+m}(a). \label{eq:cell-factorial}$$ The right side is interpreted with each finite Boolean coefficient grouped before summing over $m$. Equivalently, one may first restrict the ensemble to $N\leq M$, apply the finite identity, and then let $M\to\infty$. No assertion of absolute summability of the *ungrouped* generation series is made here.
  - Explanation: The isolated factorial density at the cell end is the grouped sum of the cell kernels applied to the larger isolated factorial densities at the cell start.
- **claim-diagramwise-factorization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cell-factorization)): Split the labels into $S=S_1\sqcup S_2$ and the roots into $R=R_1\sqcup R_2$, where $R_i\subset S_i$. Let $\gamma_i$ be a diagram on $(R_i,S_i)$. Define $\eta_I(\gamma_1,\gamma_2)$ to be the indicator that every displayed particle trajectory of $\gamma_1$ has distance greater than $\varepsilon$ throughout $I$ from every displayed trajectory of $\gamma_2$. This includes detached groups and includes the initial and terminal times. For a single global diagram $\gamma$, define $\eta_I(\gamma;S_1,S_2)$ by the same condition between its labelled paths in $S_1$ and $S_2$, using their prescribed force-group flows.* *On this indicator, merge the two root groups, and take the union of the two batches at each generation, padding a finished list by empty batches. This gives a diagram on $(R,S)$. Conversely, every nonzero diagram term satisfying $\eta_I(\gamma;S_1,S_2)=1$ splits uniquely in this manner. Its detached groups necessarily belong to one $S_i$, and its root flow splits over its two intersections with $S_i$. Under this bijection the signed transport measures, with this indicator imposed, agree with the tensor product of the two diagram transport measures: $$\sigma(\gamma)w_I(\gamma)\mathsf H^I_{R,A}\,\eta_I =\eta_I\bigl[ \sigma(\gamma_1)w_I(\gamma_1)\mathsf H^I_{R_1,A_1} \otimes \sigma(\gamma_2)w_I(\gamma_2)\mathsf H^I_{R_2,A_2} \bigr]. \label{eq:cell-factorization}$$ Summation over the bijection preserves this equality. Factorial weights split as $$\binom{m_1+m_2}{m_1} \frac{\mu^{m_1+m_2}}{(m_1+m_2)!} =\frac{\mu^{m_1}}{m_1!}\frac{\mu^{m_2}}{m_2!}. \label{eq:cell-factorial-split}$$
  - Explanation: When all displayed trajectories of two diagrams stay apart, the joint signed transport factorizes as a tensor product, and the factorial weights split accordingly.
- **claim-cell-stopping** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cell-stopping)): For $k>\|R\|$, the finite-cloud expansion equals its complete diagrams with fewer than $k$ selected labels plus a finite signed remainder. Each remainder term is of one of the following types.* 1. *Its root group $A$ already has $\|A\|\geq k$. Its transport is $c_I^R(A)\mathsf H^I_{R,A}$ times the factor $\Xi_I(\{A\};S\setminus A)\in[0,1]$.* 2. *Its completed generations and its final partial generation select a union $U$ with $\|U\|\geq k$. Immediately before the last selected component $B_*$, the number of selected labels is less than $k$. All displayed groups satisfy the connectivity, initial-polymer, same-generation avoidance, and preceding-generation touch conditions of Definition <a href="#def:cell-diagram" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:cell-diagram">11</a>. Its weight is the corresponding sign and indicators times a factor $\vartheta_I$ of the form <a href="#eq:cell-stop-theta" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cell-stop-theta">[eq:cell-stop-theta]</a>, lying in $[0,1]$.* *In the second case the overshoot is by at most one additional component. In particular, if every component of every trial subsystem formed from initial polymers has size at most $D$, then $\|A\|\leq \|R\|D$, and a stopped union has size at most $\max\{\|R\|D,k-1+D\}$.* *Replacing the sign by $+1$ and dropping the last factor gives a pointwise positive domination of the total variation of the remainder. Its expected version is a sum of these positive contact expressions integrated against the initial isolated factorial measures of the selected unions, with the factors $\mu^m/m!$ of <a href="#eq:cell-factorial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cell-factorial">[eq:cell-factorial]</a>. This is an identity and a domination statement, not a claim that this sum tends to zero.
  - Explanation: A finite cloud expansion splits into complete diagrams below a label threshold plus a signed remainder with positive domination and an overshoot of at most one component.
- **claim-centering-inversion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cent-identity)): For every $\pi$ and almost every $Z_R$, $$\label{cent:inverse} \chi_\pi^\varepsilon\widetilde F_R^\varepsilon(t) =\chi_\pi^\varepsilon \sum_{A\subset S(\pi)}f_{S(\pi)\setminus A}(t) E_{\pi_A}^\varepsilon(t).$$ Both formulas hold for arbitrary factorial densities, without a smallness or factorization assumption.
  - Explanation: Patternwise centering and its inverse are exact algebraic inverses on every pattern, for arbitrary factorial densities.
- **claim-pattern-collapse** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cent-pattern-collapse)): For $D\subset R$ let $$\label{cent:singleton-deletion} \iota_D^R(Z_R) =\prod_{i\in D}\prod_{j\in R\setminus\{i\}} \mathbf 1_{\{\|x_i-x_j\|>\varepsilon\}},\qquad \mathcal E_R=\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal P(R)}E_\pi,$$ where $\mathcal P(R)$ is the set of partitions of $R$. Then $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal E_R &=\sum_{D\subset R}(-1)^{\|D\|}\iota_D^R f_D \widetilde F_{R\setminus D}, \label{cent:collapsed-centering}\\ \widetilde F_R &=\sum_{D\subset R}\iota_D^R f_D \mathcal E_{R\setminus D}. \label{cent:collapsed-inverse} \end{aligned}$$ Thus a cut carries subset choices, but no additional factor equal to the number of partitions. For arbitrary signed families $G$ and $e$ with each $e_\pi$ supported on its pattern, write $$\\|G\\|_{1,\leq U}=\max_{0\leq r\leq U}\\|G_{[r]}\\|_1, \qquad \\|e\\|_{\pi,1,\leq U} =\max_{0\leq r\leq U}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal P([r])}\\|e_\pi\\|_1.$$ Let $\mathsf M_t$ be the transform <a href="#cent:definition-eq" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cent:definition-eq">[cent:definition-eq]</a> and $\mathsf I_t$ its inverse <a href="#cent:inverse" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cent:inverse">[cent:inverse]</a>, summed over full patterns. They satisfy $$\label{cent:centering-norm} \\|\mathsf M_tG\\|_{\pi,1,\leq U}\leq2^U\\|G\\|_{1,\leq U}, \qquad \\|\mathsf I_te\\|_{1,\leq U}\leq2^U\\|e\\|_{\pi,1,\leq U}.$$
  - Explanation: Summing over patterns collapses to subset deletions, giving norm bounds for centering and its inverse with no partition-count factor.
- **claim-short-layer-residual** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cent-residual)): Set $\beta_0=\beta/2$. There is $b_*>0$, depending only on the fixed bounds in Proposition <a href="#pre:envelopes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:envelopes">9</a>, for which the following holds. Suppose $b\leq b_*$, $K(\varepsilon)\to\infty$, and $J(\varepsilon)\to\infty$ is a fixed power of $1+\|\log\varepsilon\|$. Then $$\label{cent:residual-def} r_{u,t}^{\varepsilon}=f(t)-\mathcal G_{u,t}^{\varepsilon,J,K},\qquad \sup_{\substack{0\leq u\leq t\leq T\\t-u\leq b}} \\|r_{u,t}^{\varepsilon}\\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\beta_0} =:\eta_\varepsilon\longrightarrow0.$$ All the reference sums and residuals have uniformly bounded norms at this one fixed exponent. The proof uses only short-layer tree summability and two-body scattering.
  - Explanation: On sufficiently short layers the kinetic solution is approximated by the reference tree sum, with residual tending to zero in the summed Gaussian norm.
- **claim-simple-family-cancellation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cent-cancellation)): In the retained operator $\mathsf A_j$ of <a href="#cent:reference-mismatch" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cent:reference-mismatch">[cent:reference-mismatch]</a>, a singleton root whose displayed family satisfies the reference-history conditions $\mathcal S_n^{\varepsilon,J}$ above, ends entirely on independent $f(t_{j-1})$, and avoids every displayed trajectory of every other family throughout the layer cancels with its tensor-subtraction term. The avoidance requirement includes all detached generation groups and all reference trees coming from tensor subtraction. It also includes the full-array pattern restrictions at cuts. A residual factor is treated as a stopped slot, not as an unexamined trajectory. The pairing is exact coefficientwise for total formal size $p<K_j$. The finite complementary operator $B_j$ stays in <a href="#cent:layer-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cent:layer-identity">[cent:layer-identity]</a>; no cancellation or positive physical remainder bound is asserted for it.
  - Explanation: A simple singleton family that avoids all other displayed trajectories cancels exactly against its tensor-subtraction term for sizes below the cutoff; the finite mismatch operator remains separate.
- **claim-cross-layer-charges** (bound, [source](#stmt-cent-charges)): Consider a term remaining after Proposition <a href="#cent:cancellation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cent:cancellation">23</a>, with no unresolved cutoff interruption. Let $H$ count the root slots entering each expanded layer at its upper cut, including the starting roots and residual slots, summed over those layers. Retained inputs at time zero are terminal charges, not additional root occurrences in $H$. Let $h_0$ count uncentered restarting top roots, let $d$ be the number of distinct marked lives, let $r$ be the number of residual factors, and let $i$ be the number of retained singleton factors at time zero. With at most $L$ expanded layers, $$\label{cent:charge-bound} H\leq Lh_0+L^2d+Lr+Li.$$ In particular, if $H\geq\delta p$ and $h_0\leq\delta p/(2L)$, then $$\label{cent:extensive-charges} d+r+i\geq\frac{\delta}{2L^2}p.$$ The statement is purely combinatorial and imposes no smallness on an uncentered restarting top.
  - Explanation: A purely combinatorial count shows that many surviving root occurrences force an extensive number of marked lives, residual factors or initial factors.
- **claim-augmented-volume** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hist-gaussian)): For every fixed prescription $\Gamma$, the map from its $p$ source coordinates to $\widehat Z(t)$ is one-to-one and volume preserving outside null sets. On $\mathcal D_\Gamma$, $$\sum_{i=1}^p\|\widehat v_i(t)\|^2\leq S+2Bp, \qquad \sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\sum_{\substack{i:\,i\text{ active at }t}}\|v_i(t)\|^2 \leq S+2Bp. \label{eq:hist-mixed-energy}$$ In particular, with $V(t)=(\widehat v_i(t))_{i=1}^p$, $$e^{-\beta_0 S} \leq e^{\beta_0Bp} e^{-\beta_0\|V(t)\|^2/2}e^{-\beta_0 S/2}. \label{eq:hist-split-gaussian}$$ The same assertions hold at the last time, when the mixed coordinates consist of the top coordinates and the departure coordinates of every other label. The exponent in <a href="#eq:hist-split-gaussian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hist-split-gaussian">[eq:hist-split-gaussian]</a> is not changed at a layer cut.
  - Explanation: The map from source coordinates to augmented coordinates is volume preserving and the source energy bounds all mixed velocity sums, which yields a once-counted Gaussian weight.
- **claim-translation-charts** (construction, [source](#stmt-hist-pinning)): Consider one cell of length $\Delta$ with $q$ prescribed terminal root labels and $m$ extra labels. Products of simultaneous cell diagrams are allowed. Keep all root coordinates and all terminal velocities fixed. On the domain where every extra label is linked by the prescribed cell contacts to a root, the extra terminal positions are covered by finitely many charts with the following properties.* 1. *Each extra label costs one three-dimensional pin. A pin is either a relative endpoint displacement in $B(0,\varepsilon)$, with Jacobian one, or a contact time $t$, a normal $\omega\in\mathbb S^2$, and the Jacobian $$\varepsilon^2 \bigl\|(v_i(t)-v_j(t))\cdot\omega\bigr\|\,dt\,d\omega. \label{eq:hist-contact-jacobian}$$ The velocities in this formula are those of the separately computed translation components immediately before their merger.* 2. *The number of discrete pin charts, including a cell’s generation choices, is at most $(C(p+1))^{Cm}$. No partition of the roots is introduced. The full spatial Jacobian is the product of the pin Jacobians. No norm of a derivative of a many-particle flow occurs.* 3. *If $m=1$ globally in that cell and the extra label is separated from its own roots at the late endpoint, its first backward contact with its own tensor factor gives the exact chart $$y=x_a(t)+\varepsilon\omega+(b_I-t)w, \qquad dy\,dw=\varepsilon^2\|(w-v_a(t))\cdot\omega\| \,dt\,\,d\omega\,\,dw . \label{eq:hist-single-chart}$$ Here $b_I$ is the late endpoint, $y,w$ are the extra terminal position and velocity, and the root trajectory is computed without that extra label. Apart from the full or virtual choice in Remark <a href="#rem:cell-single" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rem:cell-single">12</a>, there is no multiplicity independent of the choice of $a$.* *On the energy shell <a href="#eq:hist-shells" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hist-shells">[eq:hist-shells]</a>, multiplication by $\mu^m$ and integration over the $m$ pin coordinates gives the coarse bound $$(Cp)^{Cm}(1+E_r)^{m/2}(\Delta+\varepsilon)^m. \label{eq:hist-multi-cell}$$ The convention $p+1$ can replace $p$ in this and subsequent bounds at zero size.
  - Explanation: Extra terminal positions are covered by triangular first-contact charts with explicit pin Jacobians and a counted number of discrete choices.
- **claim-finite-term-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-hist-basic)): Fix $A<\infty$ and the finite number $L$ of layers. There is a numerical exponent $d$ in the cell estimates such that, if $J=\lceil P^Q\rceil$ with $Q>2dA$, all the following bounds hold uniformly for $1\leq p\leq P^A$, for sufficiently small $\varepsilon$. Put $n_j$ for the total additions in layer $j$, $h_j$ for its incoming roots, and $q_j=h_j+n_j$. After summing cell choices, cell diagrams, and original parents, the absolute integral on the shell <a href="#eq:hist-shells" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hist-shells">[eq:hist-shells]</a> is bounded by $$\begin{aligned} &\eta_\Gamma C^p e^{-c rp}\mathsf S(h_0,E_r) \chi_\varepsilon^{m_*+o_*}(r+2)^{(m_*+o_*)/2} \prod_{j=1}^L\frac{b^{n_j}q_j^{n_j}}{n_j!} \nonumber\\[-2pt] &\hspace{22mm}\times \int_{\mathbb R^{3p}} \prod_{\substack{i:\,i\text{ an ordinary pin}}} \left(1+\|w_i\|^2+\frac{E_r}{q_{j(i)}}\right)^{1/2} d\gamma(V), \label{eq:hist-finite-bound} \end{aligned}$$ where $d\gamma$ is a normalized product Gaussian of one fixed exponent, the integral may retain the common indicator $\|V\|^2\leq E_r$, and one may take $$\chi_\varepsilon = C P^{dA}\left(J^{-1/2}+\frac{\varepsilon J}{b}\right). \label{eq:hist-mesh-gain}$$ Harmless increases of $d$ absorb the fixed extra powers occurring in the finite diagrams. Constants $C,c$ in <a href="#eq:hist-finite-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hist-finite-bound">[eq:hist-finite-bound]</a> do not depend on $L$ or on later rarity cutoffs. The threshold of sufficiently small $\varepsilon$ may depend on the fixed $b=T/L$.* *In particular, after summing layer occupancies and retained slots the total finite integral of order $p$ is at most $$\eta_\Gamma C_L^p. \label{eq:hist-coarse-exponential}$$ Its portion with $S>Rp$ is at most $$\eta_\Gamma C_L^p e^{-cRp}, \label{eq:hist-source-tail}$$ after changing $c>0$ and taking $R$ above a fixed constant. These are estimates of finite independently enveloped terms, not assertions about the actual process.
  - Explanation: Finite independently enveloped history integrals obey an exponential-in-size bound with an explicit mesh gain and a tail estimate for large source energy.
- **claim-parameter-discards** (bound, [source](#stmt-hist-irregular)): Fix $L$, $A$, $\delta>0$, and $M_0<\infty$. The source-energy cutoff $R$ and fixed numbers $$U,W<\infty,\qquad \gamma,\kappa>0$$ can be chosen so that, for all sufficiently small $\varepsilon$, the total finite history integral with at least $\delta p$ marked additions is at most $\eta_\Gamma e^{-M_0p}$. An addition is marked if it is in a multiple-addition cell, is an endpoint pin, or is an ordinary pin satisfying any of the following conditions:* 1. *its original parent speed exceeds $U$, its added absolute velocity exceeds $W$, or its relative speed is below $\gamma$;* 2. *for a genuine non-force-free isolated continuation, either output increment relative to the incoming parent has magnitude below $\kappa$;* 3. *its isolated two-body duration in physical time exceeds $\varepsilon^{.96}$;* 4. *its contact time is within $\varepsilon^{.95}$ of a cell boundary, or it belongs to a pair of birth times separated by less than $2\varepsilon^{.9}$.* *The contribution of $S>Rp$ has the same bound. The constants in this proposition may depend on $L$; they do not enter the $C$ in <a href="#eq:hist-finite-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hist-finite-bound">[eq:hist-finite-bound]</a>.
  - Explanation: Histories with an extensive fraction of marked additions (large speeds, grazing, long duration, close times) carry an arbitrarily strong exponential suppression.
- **claim-fixed-pivot-forest** (bound, [source](#stmt-hist-pivot)): Fix a time $t_*$, a forest of $r$ edges on the $p$ labels, and $\rho\geq\varepsilon$. For one fixed force/lifetime prescription whose required contacts connect every label to a top, the source integral before multiplication by $\mu^{p-h_0}$, on $S\leq Rp$ with $\|x_i(t_*)-x_j(t_*)\|\leq\rho$ on every forest edge is at most $$\eta_\Gamma C^p p^{Cp}\rho^{3r} \varepsilon^{2(p-r-h_0)_+}. \label{eq:hist-pivot-bound}$$ More precisely, a stratum with $k$ final translation components has the factor $\rho^{3r}\varepsilon^{2(p-r-k)}$, with $k\leq h_0$. There is no factor equal to the number of cells in this geometric bound.
  - Explanation: Source integrals under proximity constraints along a forest at a fixed time are bounded by a power of the proximity radius and of epsilon with no cell-count factor.
- **claim-extensive-crowding** (bound, [source](#stmt-hist-crowding)): Fix $A,Q,L,R$ and $\delta>0$. Among independently enveloped finite histories with $p\leq P^A$ and $S\leq Rp$, the activity-normalized integral for which at some time at least $\delta p$ displayed labels have a companion within $\rho=\varepsilon^{.85}$ is bounded by $$\eta_\Gamma P^{C_{A,Q,L,R}p} \varepsilon^{.275\delta p-1}. \label{eq:hist-crowding-explicit}$$ For $\delta p\geq8$ this is at most $\eta_\Gamma\varepsilon^{.1\delta p}$ for sufficiently small $\varepsilon$, uniformly in the stated range of $p$.
  - Explanation: An extensive simultaneous crowding event has integral bounded by a positive power of epsilon per crowded label, once the crowded number is large enough.
- **claim-separated-parents** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hist-separated)): Given $L,A,\delta>0$ and $M_0<\infty$, one can discard finite terms of total integral at most $\eta_\Gamma e^{-M_0p}$ so that, except at fewer than $\delta p$ ordinary pins, the original parent is at distance greater than $\varepsilon^{.85}$ from every other already displayed path immediately before the insertion. This assertion is used for large histories; fixed bounded orders are treated by their direct geometric estimates.
  - Explanation: After discarding exponentially small terms, nearly all ordinary insertions have an original parent well separated from every other displayed path.
- **claim-premature-contacts** (bound, [source](#stmt-exp-rerouting)): Outside the original compact-parameter, crowded-parent, and multi-addition exceptions, the contribution with a specified set of $k$ singleton cells having a premature contact has bound $$\label{exp:reroute-bound} C_L^p\bigl(p^C\varepsilon^c\bigr)^k.$$ Consequently an extensive number of premature contacts has arbitrarily strong exponential suppression. The remaining premature births may be marked irregular before selecting terminal subtrees. No global reparenting multiplicity is included or needed in this estimate.
  - Explanation: Premature contacts of singleton additions are suppressed by a positive power of epsilon each, so an extensive number is exponentially rare.
- **claim-terminal-subtrees** (theorem, [source](#stmt-exp-terminal)): Fix $0<\alpha\le1$ and an exponential accuracy $A>0$, with the layer count and polynomial size exponent $a>0$ fixed. There are constants $\theta=\theta(\alpha,A)>0$, $M<\infty$, $p_0<\infty$, and $\varepsilon_0>0$, independent of $\varepsilon$, such that the following statement holds uniformly for $$0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_0,\qquad p_0\le p\le P^a,\qquad h_0\le\theta p.$$ In the absolute history integrals with $p$ labels and $h_0$ tops, the following exclusions have total cost at most $e^{-Ap}$ times the unrestricted exponential majorant, after choosing the compact and irregularity cutoffs sufficiently strongly: there are more than $\alpha p/8$ insertion vertices whose original descendant subtree has more than $M$ vertices, or too many vertices influenced by an irregular insertion. Here $M$ is a fixed constant, chosen before $\varepsilon\downarrow0$.* *After these exclusions, if at least $\alpha p$ distinct new-particle lives have an extra contact or an uncharged defect, there are at least $c_{\alpha,M}p$ pairwise disjoint regular terminal trees, of size at most $M$, each containing a defect. For a genuine launch its selected tree contains both output branches; for a passage launch it need contain only the new branch. The selection can additionally ensure that no outside primary pin in the global coordinates attaches to a selected tree, and that the original and global genealogies agree on every selected tree.
  - Explanation: After exponentially small exclusions, extensively many defects yield a proportional family of disjoint regular bounded terminal trees each containing a defect.
- **claim-ray-tube** (bound, [source](#stmt-exp-tube)): Let $\omega$ have normalized surface measure on $\mathbb S^2$, let $0<r_0\le r\le r_1$, and let $H:[\tau,T]\to\mathbb R^3$ be independent of $\omega$ and Lipschitz with constant at most $V$. If $0<\delta\le\tau r_0/10$, then $$\label{exp:tube-bound} \int_{\mathbb S^2}\mathbf1_{\{\exists t\in[\tau,T]: \|tr\omega-H(t)\|\le\delta\}}\,d\omega \le C\frac{(V+r_1)\delta}{r_0^2\tau}.$$ For the tree consequence, fix $M\in\mathbb N$ and $a>0$, let $p\le P^a$, and retain the compact scattering, duration, birth-spacing, and boundary gates of Proposition <a href="#hist:irregular" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hist:irregular">32</a>. Test at most polynomially many exterior lifetime segments, independent of the tested launch rotation and with polynomial Lipschitz speed. Each such segment must either be tested only at elapsed times at least $\tau_\varepsilon$, or admit such an independent extension back to the launch whose point there is at distance greater than $\varepsilon^{.85}$ from the launch parent. The unchanged parent of a passage is also allowed if it is free during the initial $2\tau_\varepsilon$ interval. Prescribed scattering and passage encounters are exempt. Under these hypotheses, a regular tree of at most $M$ vertices has a first unprescribed internal or tested exterior contact with parameter measure at most $p^C\varepsilon^c$, for some $c>0$, in the unconditioned bounded-density reference measures described above.
  - Explanation: A ray with uniformly distributed direction meets an independent Lipschitz exterior curve on a set of directions of small measure; the consequence for regular trees gives a power of epsilon.
- **claim-causal-exposure** (bound, [source](#stmt-exp-exposure)): Fix an admissible mixed whole-cell prescription with $p\le P^a$ labels and a total energy budget $\mathcal K\le Rp$. Fix a collection of $k$ disjoint regular terminal trees as in Lemma <a href="#exp:terminal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="exp:terminal">37</a>. Sum the absolute activity-normalized chart integrals over the event that every selected tree has a first defect. The result is bounded by $$\label{exp:exposure-bound} \mathcal A_p\,(pJ)^{C p}\bigl(p^C\varepsilon^c\bigr)^{k/2},$$ The factor $(pJ)^{Cp}$ includes cell-segment choices. Constants may depend on the fixed layer count, compact cutoffs, $M$, and $R$.
  - Explanation: Chronologically exposing hidden launch parameters gives a geometric factor for each selected tree having a first defect.
- **claim-extensive-defect-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-exp-defects)): Fix $0<\alpha\le1$, $A>0$, the layer count, and the polynomial size exponent $a>0$. There exist fixed constants $\theta=\theta(\alpha,A)>0$, $p_0<\infty$, and sufficiently small $\varepsilon_0>0$ such that, after the compact cutoffs are chosen, the following bound holds uniformly for $$0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_0,\qquad p_0\le p\le P^a,\qquad h_0\le\theta p.$$ For histories with at least $\alpha p$ affected lives, their absolute integral, divided by the source factor $\mathcal A_p$ of <a href="#exp:source-factor" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="exp:source-factor">[exp:source-factor]</a>, is bounded by the sum of the exceptional-parameter contributions bounded by $e^{-Ap}$ and a contribution bounded by $$\label{exp:extensive} P^{C_Lp}\varepsilon^{c_Lp}.$$ The constants $c_L>0$ and $C_L$ are fixed before $\varepsilon\downarrow0$. The assertion includes recollisions, interference with a long-lived cluster, and contacts with detached or subtracted trajectories.
  - Explanation: Histories with a positive fraction of affected lives are bounded by an exponentially small exceptional part plus a polynomial-in-P power of epsilon per label.
- **claim-finite-marked-histories** (theorem, [source](#stmt-exp-finite)): Fix the layer count and an integer $p_*$. Sum the absolute integrals of the original prescribed histories with at most $p_*$ labels, integrating all top phase points, and suppose their independent source norms in <a href="#exp:source-factor" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="exp:source-factor">[exp:source-factor]</a> are uniformly bounded. The contribution with at least one unprescribed contact, an incomplete isolated scattering at a required cut, an interfering birth, or an endpoint-polymer defect tends to zero as $\varepsilon\downarrow0$. This statement includes $p=h_0$ and contacts between distinct top roots. The bounds are uniform over the observation times and shortened endpoint cells in the fixed interval. If instead a source has norm tending to zero, its finite-history integral tends to zero by the same uniform finite-size majorant.
  - Explanation: For a fixed maximal label number, the contribution of any unprescribed contact or defect tends to zero, including contacts between distinct top roots.
- **claim-slot-entropy** (bound, [source](#stmt-close-entropy)): Suppose a term retains $k_j$ slots among $q_j$ available slots at successive cuts. Put $Q=\sum_jq_j$ and $H'=\sum_jk_j$. Then $$\label{close:slot-bound} \prod_j\binom{q_j}{k_j} \leq\binom{Q}{H'} \leq\exp\left\{H'\log\frac{eQ}{H'}\right\} \quad (H'>0).$$ In the history expansion $Q\leq p+C H$ and $H'\leq C H$, up to a fixed number of choices per retained slot. Consequently, when $H\leq\delta p$, the logarithmic selection cost is at most $$\label{close:entropy-small} C\delta p\left(1+\log\frac1\delta\right).$$ For general $H\leq Lp$, its cost is at most $C_L^p$.
  - Explanation: The selection of retained slots across cuts is a binomial count whose logarithm is small when the number of layer entries is a small fraction of the labels.
- **claim-energy-factor** (bound, [source](#stmt-close-energy)): For a completed history with the above counts, the parent, time, and velocity factors in Proposition <a href="#hist:basic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hist:basic">31</a>, before retained-slot and spatial losses, have total integral at most $$\label{close:energy-factor} (C b\sqrt L)^p \exp\{H+h_0\log_+(1/b)\}, \qquad b\leq1.$$ The constant $C$ is independent of $L$. A fixed part of the source Gaussian remains available for the spatial estimates.
  - Explanation: Parent, time and velocity factors of a completed history are bounded by a per-label base proportional to the layer length times a square-root of the layer count.
- **claim-completed-summability** (bound, [source](#stmt-close-summability)): Fix any desired number $a>0$. One can choose a finite $L$, then the rarity cutoffs, with the following property. For every fixed polynomial label cap $p\leq P^A$, choose $J$ to be a sufficiently large fixed power of $P$. There exist $\eta_L>0$ and $p_L<\infty$ such that, for sufficiently small $\varepsilon$, the sum of the integrated absolute weights of completed histories with exactly $p$ labels is at most $$\label{close:complete-tail} e^{-ap},\qquad p\geq p_L,\qquad h_0\leq\eta_Lp.$$ The same bound holds for an admissible mixed uncentered upper block followed by centered lower layers, after the lower simple-family cancellations. It is uniform over shortened endpoint cells and layers and over tests of absolute value at most one. For the original centered expansion, in which the upper simple-family cancellations are also made, every fixed-size term with a retained centered upper root tends to zero after integration.
  - Explanation: For suitable layer count and cutoffs, completed histories with many labels have total absolute weight exponentially small in the label number.
- **claim-witness-extraction** (construction, [source](#stmt-close-witness-extraction)): Let $D\geq1$ be an integer. For a finite microscopic cloud, a first occurrence of $\mathcal B_j(D)$ admits a witness in some layer $i\leq j$ with one distinguished integrated upper label and between $D$ and $2D^2$ formal labels. The witness consists of positive actual component histories before its last cell and one trial subsystem in its last cell. Every starting or departing set respects whole instantaneous polymers. The bound is independent of the cutoff assigned separately to any earlier layer. The weighted ancestral construction above in fact permits $D<p\leq2D$.
  - Explanation: A first occurrence of an oversized ancestral cluster yields a bounded positive witness history with one distinguished upper label.
- **claim-raw-amplification** (bound, [source](#stmt-close-amplification)): Consider a finite completed block containing at most $M$ labels and acting on an input signed array in integrated total variation. For $M$ and $J$ bounded by fixed powers of $P$, its operator norm is at most $$\label{close:raw-amplification} \exp(C_LMP).$$ The same bound holds for the pattern-summed centered norm of <a href="#cent:centering-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cent:centering-norm">[cent:centering-norm]</a>. If a physical layer is stopped below $K$ labels, the contribution of a microscopic event $\mathcal A$ to the exact observable minus its completed physical below-$K$ expansion is at most $$\label{close:bad-discard} \exp(C_LKP)\,\mathbb P(\mathcal A)^{1/2},$$ provided its upper root count is less than $K$. This discard is made before estimating any overshoot.
  - Explanation: A finite completed block of bounded size has an exponential-in-size operator norm, and bad microscopic events are discarded with a square-root probability cost.
- **claim-actual-tails** (bound, [source](#stmt-close-tails)): With the preceding schedule, for sufficiently small $\varepsilon$ and every $1\leq j\leq L$, $$\label{close:actual-tail-bounds} \mathbb P\bigl(\mathcal B_j(D_j)\bigr)\leq e^{-8D_j}, \qquad R_j\leq e^{-8K_j}.$$ The estimates use the original Newtonian process, without conditioning it to stay in a good set.
  - Explanation: By chronological induction over layers, oversized ancestral clusters are exponentially improbable and the layer remainders are exponentially small, without conditioning the process.

        "anchor": "stmt-pre-scattering",
        "exact_quote": "Outside a set of zero incoming flux, an encounter either is a straight force-free passage or satisfies $$\\label{eq:pre-increments}\n v'\\ne v,\\qquad v_*'\\ne v .$$ For $M<\\infty$ and $\\eta>0$, the non-free relative incoming data $(n,g)$ with $|g|\\leq M$ can be restricted to a rotation-invariant compact set, losing at most $\\eta$ relative incoming flux, on which the duration is bounded and $|g|$, $|v'-v|$, and $|v_*'-v|$ have positive lower bounds.         "exact_quote": "Let $R\\subset S$ and let $A_0$ be the union of the blocks of $\\pi_0(S)$ meeting $R$. Then $$\\mathsf H^I_{R,S}\n =\\sum_{\\mathcal U\\subset\\mathcal P}\n      \\mathsf K^I_{R,A_0\\cup\\bigcup\\mathcal U},\n \\label{eq:cell-subset-sum}$$ and, as an equality of signed measures, $$\\mathsf K^I_{R,A_0\\cup\\bigcup\\mathcal U}\n  =\\sum_{\\mathcal V\\subset\\mathcal U}\n       (-1)^{|\\mathcal U|-|\\mathcal V|}\n       \\mathsf H^I_{R,A_0\\cup\\bigcup\\mathcal V}.\n \\label{eq:cell-boolean}$$ Every coefficient is evaluated on its displayed input coordinates.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cell-factorization",
        "exact_quote": "Split the labels into $S=S_1\\sqcup S_2$ and the roots into $R=R_1\\sqcup R_2$, where $R_i\\subset S_i$. Let $\\gamma_i$ be a diagram on $(R_i,S_i)$. Define $\\eta_I(\\gamma_1,\\gamma_2)$ to be the indicator that every displayed particle trajectory of $\\gamma_1$ has distance greater than $\\varepsilon$ throughout $I$ from every displayed trajectory of $\\gamma_2$. This includes detached groups and includes the initial and terminal times. For a single global diagram $\\gamma$, define $\\eta_I(\\gamma;S_1,S_2)$ by the same condition between its labelled paths in $S_1$ and $S_2$, using their prescribed force-group flows.*\n\n\n\n*On this indicator, merge the two root groups, and take the union of the two batches at each generation, padding a finished list by empty batches. This gives a diagram on $(R,S)$. Conversely, every nonzero diagram term satisfying $\\eta_I(\\gamma;S_1,S_2)=1$ splits uniquely in this manner. Its detached groups necessarily belong to one $S_i$, and its root flow splits over its two intersections with $S_i$. Under this bijection the signed transport measures, with this indicator imposed, agree with the tensor product of the two diagram transport measures: $$\\sigma(\\gamma)w_I(\\gamma)\\mathsf H^I_{R,A}\\,\\eta_I\n  =\\eta_I\\bigl[\n       \\sigma(\\gamma_1)w_I(\\gamma_1)\\mathsf H^I_{R_1,A_1}\n       \\otimes\n       \\sigma(\\gamma_2)w_I(\\gamma_2)\\mathsf H^I_{R_2,A_2}\n             \\bigr].\n \\label{eq:cell-factorization}$$ Summation over the bijection preserves this equality. Factorial weights split as $$\\binom{m_1+m_2}{m_1}\n       \\frac{\\mu^{m_1+m_2}}{(m_1+m_2)!}\n   =\\frac{\\mu^{m_1}}{m_1!}\\frac{\\mu^{m_2}}{m_2!}.\n \\label{eq:cell-factorial-split}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-364.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cell-factorization",
          "exact_quote": "Split the labels into $S=S_1\\sqcup S_2$ and the roots into $R=R_1\\sqcup R_2$, where $R_i\\subset S_i$. Let $\\gamma_i$ be a diagram on $(R_i,S_i)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cell-stopping",
        "exact_quote": "For $k>|R|$, the finite-cloud expansion equals its complete diagrams with fewer than $k$ selected labels plus a finite signed remainder. Each remainder term is of one of the following types.*\n\n1.  *Its root group $A$ already has $|A|\\geq k$. Its transport is $c_I^R(A)\\mathsf H^I_{R,A}$ times the factor $\\Xi_I(\\{A\\};S\\setminus A)\\in[0,1]$.*\n\n2.  *Its completed generations and its final partial generation select a union $U$ with $|U|\\geq k$. Immediately before the last selected component $B_*$, the number of selected labels is less than $k$. All displayed groups satisfy the connectivity, initial-polymer, same-generation avoidance, and preceding-generation touch conditions of Definition <a href=\"#def:cell-diagram\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:cell-diagram\">11</a>. Its weight is the corresponding sign and indicators times a factor $\\vartheta_I$ of the form <a href=\"#eq:cell-stop-theta\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cell-stop-theta\">[eq:cell-stop-theta]</a>, lying in $[0,1]$.*\n\n*In the second case the overshoot is by at most one additional component. In particular, if every component of every trial subsystem formed from initial polymers has size at most $D$, then $|A|\\leq |R|D$, and a stopped union has size at most $\\max\\{|R|D,k-1+D\\}$.*\n\n*Replacing the sign by $+1$ and dropping the last factor gives a pointwise positive domination of the total variation of the remainder. Its expected version is a sum of these positive contact expressions integrated against the initial isolated factorial measures of the selected unions, with the factors $\\mu^m/m!$ of <a href=\"#eq:cell-factorial\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cell-factorial\">[eq:cell-factorial]</a>. This is an identity and a domination statement, not a claim that this sum tends to zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cent-identity",
        "exact_quote": "For every $\\pi$ and almost every $Z_R$, $$\\label{cent:inverse}\n \\chi_\\pi^\\varepsilon\\widetilde F_R^\\varepsilon(t)\n =\\chi_\\pi^\\varepsilon\n \\sum_{A\\subset S(\\pi)}f_{S(\\pi)\\setminus A}(t)\n E_{\\pi_A}^\\varepsilon(t).$$ Both formulas hold for arbitrary factorial densities, without a smallness or factorization assumption.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cent-identity",
          "exact_quote": "For every $\\pi$ and almost every $Z_R$, $$\\label{cent:inverse}\n \\chi_\\pi^\\varepsilon\\widetilde F_R^\\varepsilon(t)\n =\\chi_\\pi^\\varepsilon\n \\sum_{A\\subset S(\\pi)}f_{S(\\pi)\\setminus A}(t)\n E_{\\pi_A}^\\varepsilon(t).$$ Both formulas hold for arbitrary factorial densities, without a smallness or factorization assumption.",
          "source_id": "PM-364.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every $\\pi$ and almost every $Z_R$, $$\\label{cent:inverse}\n \\chi_\\pi^\\varepsilon\\widetilde F_R^\\varepsilon(t)\n =\\chi_\\pi^\\varepsilon\n \\sum_{A\\subset S(\\pi)}f_{S(\\pi)\\setminus A}(t)\n E_{\\pi_A}^\\varepsilon(t).$$ Both formulas hold for arbitrary factorial densities, without a smallness or factorization assumption.",
      "explanation": "Patternwise centering and its inverse are exact algebraic inverses on every pattern, for arbitrary factorial densities.",

      "hypotheses": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cent-pattern-collapse",
        "exact_quote": "For $D\\subset R$ let $$\\label{cent:singleton-deletion}\n \\iota_D^R(Z_R)\n =\\prod_{i\\in D}\\prod_{j\\in R\\setminus\\{i\\}}\n       \\mathbf 1_{\\{|x_i-x_j|>\\varepsilon\\}},\\qquad\n \\mathcal E_R=\\sum_{\\pi\\in\\mathcal P(R)}E_\\pi,$$ where $\\mathcal P(R)$ is the set of partitions of $R$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal E_R\n &=\\sum_{D\\subset R}(-1)^{|D|}\\iota_D^R f_D\n                       \\widetilde F_{R\\setminus D},\n       \\label{cent:collapsed-centering}\\\\\n \\widetilde F_R\n &=\\sum_{D\\subset R}\\iota_D^R f_D\n                       \\mathcal E_{R\\setminus D}.\n       \\label{cent:collapsed-inverse}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Thus a cut carries subset choices, but no additional factor equal to the number of partitions. For arbitrary signed families $G$ and $e$ with each $e_\\pi$ supported on its pattern, write $$\\|G\\|_{1,\\leq U}=\\max_{0\\leq r\\leq U}\\|G_{[r]}\\|_1,\n \\qquad\n \\|e\\|_{\\pi,1,\\leq U}\n   =\\max_{0\\leq r\\leq U}\\sum_{\\pi\\in\\mathcal P([r])}\\|e_\\pi\\|_1.$$ Let $\\mathsf M_t$ be the transform <a href=\"#cent:definition-eq\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cent:definition-eq\">[cent:definition-eq]</a> and $\\mathsf I_t$ its inverse <a href=\"#cent:inverse\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cent:inverse\">[cent:inverse]</a>, summed over full patterns. They satisfy $$\\label{cent:centering-norm}\n \\|\\mathsf M_tG\\|_{\\pi,1,\\leq U}\\leq2^U\\|G\\|_{1,\\leq U},\n \\qquad\n \\|\\mathsf I_te\\|_{1,\\leq U}\\leq2^U\\|e\\|_{\\pi,1,\\leq U}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-364.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cent-pattern-collapse",

        "exact_quote": "Set $\\beta_0=\\beta/2$. There is $b_*>0$, depending only on the fixed bounds in Proposition <a href=\"#pre:envelopes\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"pre:envelopes\">9</a>, for which the following holds. Suppose $b\\leq b_*$, $K(\\varepsilon)\\to\\infty$, and $J(\\varepsilon)\\to\\infty$ is a fixed power of $1+|\\log\\varepsilon|$. Then $$\\label{cent:residual-def}\n r_{u,t}^{\\varepsilon}=f(t)-\\mathcal G_{u,t}^{\\varepsilon,J,K},\\qquad\n \\sup_{\\substack{0\\leq u\\leq t\\leq T\\\\t-u\\leq b}}\n \\|r_{u,t}^{\\varepsilon}\\|_{\\mathrm{Bol},\\beta_0}\n =:\\eta_\\varepsilon\\longrightarrow0.$$ All the reference sums and residuals have uniformly bounded norms at this one fixed exponent.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cent-cancellation",
        "exact_quote": "In the retained operator $\\mathsf A_j$ of <a href=\"#cent:reference-mismatch\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cent:reference-mismatch\">[cent:reference-mismatch]</a>, a singleton root whose displayed family satisfies the reference-history conditions $\\mathcal S_n^{\\varepsilon,J}$ above, ends entirely on independent $f(t_{j-1})$, and avoids every displayed trajectory of every other family throughout the layer cancels with its tensor-subtraction term. The avoidance requirement includes all detached generation groups and all reference trees coming from tensor subtraction. It also includes the full-array pattern restrictions at cuts. A residual factor is treated as a stopped slot, not as an unexamined trajectory. The pairing is exact coefficientwise for total formal size $p<K_j$. The finite complementary operator $B_j$ stays in <a href=\"#cent:layer-identity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cent:layer-identity\">[cent:layer-identity]</a>; no cancellation or positive physical remainder bound is asserted for it.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cent-charges",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a term remaining after Proposition <a href=\"#cent:cancellation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cent:cancellation\">23</a>, with no unresolved cutoff interruption. Let $H$ count the root slots entering each expanded layer at its upper cut, including the starting roots and residual slots, summed over those layers. Retained inputs at time zero are terminal charges, not additional root occurrences in $H$. Let $h_0$ count uncentered restarting top roots, let $d$ be the number of distinct marked lives, let $r$ be the number of residual factors, and let $i$ be the number of retained singleton factors at time zero. With at most $L$ expanded layers, $$\\label{cent:charge-bound}\n H\\leq Lh_0+L^2d+Lr+Li.$$ In particular, if $H\\geq\\delta p$ and $h_0\\leq\\delta p/(2L)$, then $$\\label{cent:extensive-charges}\n d+r+i\\geq\\frac{\\delta}{2L^2}p.$$ The statement is purely combinatorial and imposes no smallness on an uncentered restarting top.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cent-charges",
          "exact_quote": "Consider a term remaining after Proposition <a href=\"#cent:cancellation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cent:cancellation\">23</a>, with no unresolved cutoff interruption. Retained inputs at time zero are terminal charges, not additional root occurrences in $H$. Let $h_0$ count uncentered restarting top roots, let $d$ be the number of distinct marked lives, let $r$ be the number of residual factors, and let $i$ be the number of retained singleton factors at time zero. With at most $L$ expanded layers, $$\\label{cent:charge-bound}\n H\\leq Lh_0+L^2d+Lr+Li.$$ In particular, if $H\\geq\\delta p$ and $h_0\\leq\\delta p/(2L)$, then $$\\label{cent:extensive-charges}\n d+r+i\\geq\\frac{\\delta}{2L^2}p.$$ The statement is purely combinatorial and imposes no smallness on an uncentered restarting top.",

With at most $L$ expanded layers, $$\\label{cent:charge-bound}\n H\\leq Lh_0+L^2d+Lr+Li.$$ In particular, if $H\\geq\\delta p$ and $h_0\\leq\\delta p/(2L)$, then $$\\label{cent:extensive-charges}\n d+r+i\\geq\\frac{\\delta}{2L^2}p.$$ The statement is purely combinatorial and imposes no smallness on an uncentered restarting top.",
      "explanation": "A purely combinatorial count shows that many surviving root occurrences force an extensive number of marked lives, residual factors or initial factors.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-hist-gaussian",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed prescription $\\Gamma$, the map from its $p$ source coordinates to $\\widehat Z(t)$ is one-to-one and volume preserving outside null sets. On $\\mathcal D_\\Gamma$, $$\\sum_{i=1}^p|\\widehat v_i(t)|^2\\leq S+2Bp,\n \\qquad\n \\sup_{0\\leq t\\leq T}\\sum_{\\substack{i:\\,i\\text{ active at }t}}|v_i(t)|^2\n       \\leq S+2Bp.\n \\label{eq:hist-mixed-energy}$$ In particular, with $V(t)=(\\widehat v_i(t))_{i=1}^p$, $$e^{-\\beta_0 S}\n \\leq e^{\\beta_0Bp}\n       e^{-\\beta_0|V(t)|^2/2}e^{-\\beta_0 S/2}.\n \\label{eq:hist-split-gaussian}$$ The same assertions hold at the last time, when the mixed coordinates consist of the top coordinates and the departure coordinates of every other label. The exponent in <a href=\"#eq:hist-split-gaussian\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:hist-split-gaussian\">[eq:hist-split-gaussian]</a> is not changed at a layer cut.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-hist-pinning",
        "exact_quote": "Consider one cell of length $\\Delta$ with $q$ prescribed terminal root labels and $m$ extra labels. Products of simultaneous cell diagrams are allowed. Keep all root coordinates and all terminal velocities fixed. On the domain where every extra label is linked by the prescribed cell contacts to a root, the extra terminal positions are covered by finitely many charts with the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *Each extra label costs one three-dimensional pin. A pin is either a relative endpoint displacement in $B(0,\\varepsilon)$, with Jacobian one, or a contact time $t$, a normal $\\omega\\in\\mathbb S^2$, and the Jacobian $$\\varepsilon^2\n     \\bigl|(v_i(t)-v_j(t))\\cdot\\omega\\bigr|\\,dt\\,d\\omega.\n     \\label{eq:hist-contact-jacobian}$$ The velocities in this formula are those of the separately computed translation components immediately before their merger.*\n\n2.  *The number of discrete pin charts, including a cell’s generation choices, is at most $(C(p+1))^{Cm}$. No partition of the roots is introduced. The full spatial Jacobian is the product of the pin Jacobians. No norm of a derivative of a many-particle flow occurs.*\n\n\n3.  *If $m=1$ globally in that cell and the extra label is separated from its own roots at the late endpoint, its first backward contact with its own tensor factor gives the exact chart $$y=x_a(t)+\\varepsilon\\omega+(b_I-t)w,\n     \\qquad\n     dy\\,dw=\\varepsilon^2|(w-v_a(t))\\cdot\\omega|\n              \\,dt\\,\\,d\\omega\\,\\,dw .\n     \\label{eq:hist-single-chart}$$ Here $b_I$ is the late endpoint, $y,w$ are the extra terminal position and velocity, and the root trajectory is computed without that extra label. Apart from the full or virtual choice in Remark <a href=\"#rem:cell-single\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rem:cell-single\">12</a>, there is no multiplicity independent of the choice of $a$.*\n\n\n*On the energy shell <a href=\"#eq:hist-shells\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:hist-shells\">[eq:hist-shells]</a>, multiplication by $\\mu^m$ and integration over the $m$ pin coordinates gives the coarse bound $$(Cp)^{Cm}(1+E_r)^{m/2}(\\Delta+\\varepsilon)^m.\n \\label{eq:hist-multi-cell}$$ The convention $p+1$ can replace $p$ in this and subsequent bounds at zero size.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

Apart from the full or virtual choice in Remark <a href=\"#rem:cell-single\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"rem:cell-single\">12</a>, there is no multiplicity independent of the choice of $a$.*\n\n\n*On the energy shell <a href=\"#eq:hist-shells\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:hist-shells\">[eq:hist-shells]</a>, multiplication by $\\mu^m$ and integration over the $m$ pin coordinates gives the coarse bound $$(Cp)^{Cm}(1+E_r)^{m/2}(\\Delta+\\varepsilon)^m.\n \\label{eq:hist-multi-cell}$$ The convention $p+1$ can replace $p$ in this and subsequent bounds at zero size.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-hist-basic",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $A<\\infty$ and the finite number $L$ of layers. There is a numerical exponent $d$ in the cell estimates such that, if $J=\\lceil P^Q\\rceil$ with $Q>2dA$, all the following bounds hold uniformly for $1\\leq p\\leq P^A$, for sufficiently small $\\varepsilon$. Put $n_j$ for the total additions in layer $j$, $h_j$ for its incoming roots, and $q_j=h_j+n_j$. After summing cell choices, cell diagrams, and original parents, the absolute integral on the shell <a href=\"#eq:hist-shells\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:hist-shells\">[eq:hist-shells]</a> is bounded by $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\eta_\\Gamma C^p e^{-c rp}\\mathsf S(h_0,E_r)\n       \\chi_\\varepsilon^{m_*+o_*}(r+2)^{(m_*+o_*)/2}\n       \\prod_{j=1}^L\\frac{b^{n_j}q_j^{n_j}}{n_j!}\n \\nonumber\\\\[-2pt]\n &\\hspace{22mm}\\times\n  \\int_{\\mathbb R^{3p}}\n       \\prod_{\\substack{i:\\,i\\text{ an ordinary pin}}}\n          \\left(1+|w_i|^2+\\frac{E_r}{q_{j(i)}}\\right)^{1/2}\n       d\\gamma(V),\n \\label{eq:hist-finite-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $d\\gamma$ is a normalized product Gaussian of one fixed exponent, the integral may retain the common indicator $|V|^2\\leq E_r$, and one may take $$\\chi_\\varepsilon\n   = C P^{dA}\\left(J^{-1/2}+\\frac{\\varepsilon J}{b}\\right).\n \\label{eq:hist-mesh-gain}$$ Harmless increases of $d$ absorb the fixed extra powers occurring in the finite diagrams. Constants $C,c$ in <a href=\"#eq:hist-finite-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:hist-finite-bound\">[eq:hist-finite-bound]</a> do not depend on $L$ or on later rarity cutoffs. The threshold of sufficiently small $\\varepsilon$ may depend on the fixed $b=T/L$.*\n\n\n\n*In particular, after summing layer occupancies and retained slots the total finite integral of order $p$ is at most $$\\eta_\\Gamma C_L^p.\n \\label{eq:hist-coarse-exponential}$$ Its portion with $S>Rp$ is at most $$\\eta_\\Gamma C_L^p e^{-cRp},\n \\label{eq:hist-source-tail}$$ after changing $c>0$ and taking $R$ above a fixed constant. These are estimates of finite independently enveloped terms, not assertions about the actual process.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-hist-irregular",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $L$, $A$, $\\delta>0$, and $M_0<\\infty$. The source-energy cutoff $R$ and fixed numbers $$U,W<\\infty,\\qquad \\gamma,\\kappa>0$$ can be chosen so that, for all sufficiently small $\\varepsilon$, the total finite history integral with at least $\\delta p$ marked additions is at most $\\eta_\\Gamma e^{-M_0p}$. An addition is marked if it is in a multiple-addition cell, is an endpoint pin, or is an ordinary pin satisfying any of the following conditions:*\n\n1.  *its original parent speed exceeds $U$, its added absolute velocity exceeds $W$, or its relative speed is below $\\gamma$;*\n\n2.  *for a genuine non-force-free isolated continuation, either output increment relative to the incoming parent has magnitude below $\\kappa$;*\n\n3.  *its isolated two-body duration in physical time exceeds $\\varepsilon^{.96}$;*\n\n4.  *its contact time is within $\\varepsilon^{.95}$ of a cell boundary, or it belongs to a pair of birth times separated by less than $2\\varepsilon^{.9}$.*\n\n*The contribution of $S>Rp$ has the same bound. The constants in this proposition may depend on $L$; they do not enter the $C$ in <a href=\"#eq:hist-finite-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:hist-finite-bound\">[eq:hist-finite-bound]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-hist-irregular",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $L$, $A$, $\\delta>0$, and $M_0<\\infty$. An addition is marked if it is in a multiple-addition cell, is an endpoint pin, or is an ordinary pin satisfying any of the following conditions:*\n\n1.  *its original parent speed exceeds $U$, its added absolute velocity exceeds $W$, or its relative speed is below $\\gamma$;*\n\n2.  *for a genuine non-force-free isolated continuation, either output increment relative to the incoming parent has magnitude below $\\kappa$;*\n\n3.  *its isolated two-body duration in physical time exceeds $\\varepsilon^{.96}$;*\n\n4.  *its contact time is within $\\varepsilon^{.95}$ of a cell boundary, or it belongs to a pair of birth times separated by less than $2\\varepsilon^{.9}$.*\n\n*The contribution of $S>Rp$ has the same bound. The constants in this proposition may depend on $L$; they do not enter the $C$ in <a href=\"#eq:hist-finite-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:hist-finite-bound\">[eq:hist-finite-bound]</a>.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-hist-pivot",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a time $t_*$, a forest of $r$ edges on the $p$ labels, and $\\rho\\geq\\varepsilon$. For one fixed force/lifetime prescription whose required contacts connect every label to a top, the source integral before multiplication by $\\mu^{p-h_0}$, on $S\\leq Rp$ with $|x_i(t_*)-x_j(t_*)|\\leq\\rho$ on every forest edge is at most $$\\eta_\\Gamma C^p p^{Cp}\\rho^{3r}\n       \\varepsilon^{2(p-r-h_0)_+}.\n \\label{eq:hist-pivot-bound}$$ More precisely, a stratum with $k$ final translation components has the factor $\\rho^{3r}\\varepsilon^{2(p-r-k)}$, with $k\\leq h_0$.         "exact_quote": "Fix $A,Q,L,R$ and $\\delta>0$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-exp-terminal",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\alpha\\le1$ and an exponential accuracy $A>0$, with the layer count and polynomial size exponent $a>0$ fixed. There are constants $\\theta=\\theta(\\alpha,A)>0$, $M<\\infty$, $p_0<\\infty$, and $\\varepsilon_0>0$, independent of $\\varepsilon$, such that the following statement holds uniformly for $$0<\\varepsilon<\\varepsilon_0,\\qquad p_0\\le p\\le P^a,\\qquad h_0\\le\\theta p.$$ In the absolute history integrals with $p$ labels and $h_0$ tops, the following exclusions have total cost at most $e^{-Ap}$ times the unrestricted exponential majorant, after choosing the compact and irregularity cutoffs sufficiently strongly: there are more than $\\alpha p/8$ insertion vertices whose original descendant subtree has more than $M$ vertices, or too many vertices influenced by an irregular insertion. Here $M$ is a fixed constant, chosen before $\\varepsilon\\downarrow0$.*\n\n*After these exclusions, if at least $\\alpha p$ distinct new-particle lives have an extra contact or an uncharged defect, there are at least $c_{\\alpha,M}p$ pairwise disjoint regular terminal trees, of size at most $M$, each containing a defect. For a genuine launch its selected tree contains both output branches; for a passage launch it need contain only the new branch. The selection can additionally ensure that no outside primary pin in the global coordinates attaches to a selected tree, and that the original and global genealogies agree on every selected tree.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-exp-terminal",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $0<\\alpha\\le1$ and an exponential accuracy $A>0$, with the layer count and polynomial size exponent $a>0$ fixed. Here $M$ is a fixed constant, chosen before $\\varepsilon\\downarrow0$.*\n\n*After these exclusions, if at least $\\alpha p$ distinct new-particle lives have an extra contact or an uncharged defect, there are at least $c_{\\alpha,M}p$ pairwise disjoint regular terminal trees, of size at most $M$, each containing a defect. For a genuine launch its selected tree contains both output branches; for a passage launch it need contain only the new branch. The selection can additionally ensure that no outside primary pin in the global coordinates attaches to a selected tree, and that the original and global genealogies agree on every selected tree.",
          "source_id": "PM-364.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $0<\\alpha\\le1$ and an exponential accuracy $A>0$, with the layer count and polynomial size exponent $a>0$ fixed. Here $M$ is a fixed constant, chosen before $\\varepsilon\\downarrow0$.*\n\n*After these exclusions, if at least $\\alpha p$ distinct new-particle lives have an extra contact or an uncharged defect, there are at least $c_{\\alpha,M}p$ pairwise disjoint regular terminal trees, of size at most $M$, each containing a defect. The selection can additionally ensure that no outside primary pin in the global coordinates attaches to a selected tree, and that the original and global genealogies agree on every selected tree.",
      "explanation": "After exponentially small exclusions, extensively many defects yield a proportional family of disjoint regular bounded terminal trees each containing a defect.",

      "id": "claim-terminal-subtrees",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-exp-tube",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\omega$ have normalized surface measure on $\\mathbb S^2$, let $0<r_0\\le r\\le r_1$, and let $H:[\\tau,T]\\to\\mathbb R^3$ be independent of $\\omega$ and Lipschitz with constant at most $V$. If $0<\\delta\\le\\tau r_0/10$, then $$\\label{exp:tube-bound}\n \\int_{\\mathbb S^2}\\mathbf1_{\\{\\exists t\\in[\\tau,T]:\n |tr\\omega-H(t)|\\le\\delta\\}}\\,d\\omega\n \\le C\\frac{(V+r_1)\\delta}{r_0^2\\tau}.$$ For the tree consequence, fix $M\\in\\mathbb N$ and $a>0$, let $p\\le P^a$, and retain the compact scattering, duration, birth-spacing, and boundary gates of Proposition <a href=\"#hist:irregular\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"hist:irregular\">32</a>. Test at most polynomially many exterior lifetime segments, independent of the tested launch rotation and with polynomial Lipschitz speed. Each such segment must either be tested only at elapsed times at least $\\tau_\\varepsilon$, or admit such an independent extension back to the launch whose point there is at distance greater than $\\varepsilon^{.85}$ from the launch parent. The unchanged parent of a passage is also allowed if it is free during the initial $2\\tau_\\varepsilon$ interval. Prescribed scattering and passage encounters are exempt. Under these hypotheses, a regular tree of at most $M$ vertices has a first unprescribed internal or tested exterior contact with parameter measure at most $p^C\\varepsilon^c$, for some $c>0$, in the unconditioned bounded-density reference measures described above.",
        "source_id": "PM-364.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-exp-tube",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\omega$ have normalized surface measure on $\\mathbb S^2$, let $0<r_0\\le r\\le r_1$, and let $H:[\\tau,T]\\to\\mathbb R^3$ be independent of $\\omega$ and Lipschitz with constant at most $V$. If $0<\\delta\\le\\tau r_0/10$, then $$\\label{exp:tube-bound}\n \\int_{\\mathbb S^2}\\mathbf1_{\\{\\exists t\\in[\\tau,T]:\n |tr\\omega-H(t)|\\le\\delta\\}}\\,d\\omega\n \\le C\\frac{(V+r_1)\\delta}{r_0^2\\tau}.$$ For the tree consequence, fix $M\\in\\mathbb N$ and $a>0$, let $p\\le P^a$, and retain the compact scattering, duration, birth-spacing, and boundary gates of Proposition <a href=\"#hist:irregular\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"hist:irregular\">32</a>. Test at most polynomially many exterior lifetime segments, independent of the tested launch rotation and with polynomial Lipschitz speed. Each such segment must either be tested only at elapsed times at least $\\tau_\\varepsilon$, or admit such an independent extension back to the launch whose point there is at distance greater than $\\varepsilon^{.85}$ from the launch parent. The unchanged parent of a passage is also allowed if it is free during the initial $2\\tau_\\varepsilon$ interval. Prescribed scattering and passage encounters are exempt. Under these hypotheses, a regular tree of at most $M$ vertices has a first unprescribed internal or tested exterior contact with parameter measure at most $p^C\\varepsilon^c$, for some $c>0$, in the unconditioned bounded-density reference measures described above.",
          "source_id": "PM-364.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\omega$ have normalized surface measure on $\\mathbb S^2$, let $0<r_0\\le r\\le r_1$, and let $H:[\\tau,T]\\to\\mathbb R^3$ be independent of $\\omega$ and Lipschitz with constant at most $V$. If $0<\\delta\\le\\tau r_0/10$, then $$\\label{exp:tube-bound}\n \\int_{\\mathbb S^2}\\mathbf1_{\\{\\exists t\\in[\\tau,T]:\n |tr\\omega-H(t)|\\le\\delta\\}}\\,d\\omega\n \\le C\\frac{(V+r_1)\\delta}{r_0^2\\tau}.$$ For the tree consequence, fix $M\\in\\mathbb N$ and $a>0$, let $p\\le P^a$, and retain the compact scattering, duration, birth-spacing, and boundary gates of Proposition <a href=\"#hist:irregular\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"hist:irregular\">32</a>. Test at most polynomially many exterior lifetime segments, independent of the tested launch rotation and with polynomial Lipschitz speed. Each such segment must either be tested only at elapsed times at least $\\tau_\\varepsilon$, or admit such an independent extension back to the launch whose point there is at distance greater than $\\varepsilon^{.85}$ from the launch parent. The unchanged parent of a passage is also allowed if it is free during the initial $2\\tau_\\varepsilon$ interval. Prescribed scattering and passage encounters are exempt. Under these hypotheses, a regular tree of at most $M$ vertices has a first unprescribed internal or tested exterior contact with parameter measure at most $p^C\\varepsilon^c$, for some $c>0$, in the unconditioned bounded-density reference measures described above.",
      "explanation": "A ray with uniformly distributed direction meets an independent Lipschitz exterior curve on a set of directions of small measure; the consequence for regular trees gives a power of epsilon.",
