<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **summed spatial Gaussian norm** ([source](#intro:norm)): Write $z=(x,v)\in\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3$. For a phase-space function $h$ and $\gamma>0$, write $$\label{intro:norm} \\|h\\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\gamma} :=\sum_{k\in\mathbb Z^3}\; \sup_{\substack{\|x-k\|\le1\\v\in\mathbb R^3}} e^{\gamma\|v\|^2}\|h(x,v)\|.$$ For a vector-valued function we use its Euclidean length inside this definition. The precise potential class, grand-canonical law, Newton equations, and scattering operator are given in Assumption <a href="#pre:potential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:potential">1</a> and Equations <a href="#pre:model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:model">[pre:model]</a>, <a href="#pre:dynamics" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:dynamics">[pre:dynamics]</a>, and <a href="#pre:collision" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:collision">[pre:collision]</a>. In brief, the potential is radial, vanishes beyond unit distance, and is $C^2$, with either a finite value or a repulsive singularity at the origin. Its only sign condition is thermodynamic stability, in the classical sense of (Ruelle 1969) [[Ruelle69](#ref-Ruelle69)]. The initial law is the product activity $f_0$ conditioned by pairwise exclusion at distance $\varepsilon$, with its exact grand-canonical normalization. For each fixed integer $s\ge1$, the scaled factorial density $F_s^\varepsilon(t)$ is characterized by $$\label{intro:factorial-test} \int a(Z_s)F_s^\varepsilon(t,Z_s)\,dZ_s =\mu_\varepsilon^{-s}\mathbb E \sum_{\substack{1\le i_1,\ldots,i_s\le N\\ i_1,\ldots,i_s\ \mathrm{distinct}}} a(z_{i_1}(t),\ldots,z_{i_s}(t))$$ for bounded measurable tests $a$ on $(\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3)^s$. The sum runs over ordered distinct labels and is zero when $N<s$; $Z_s=(z_1,\ldots,z_s)$ denotes an ordered phase-space configuration.
- **potential class** ([source](#stmt-pre-potential)): Write $\Phi(q)=\phi(\|q\|)$, with $\Phi(q)=0$ for $\|q\|\geq1$. Assume either $\Phi\in C^2(\mathbb R^3;\mathbb R)$, or $\Phi\in C^2(\mathbb R^3\setminus\{0\})$ with $\Phi(q)\to+\infty$ as $q\to0$. In the latter case set $\Phi(0)=+\infty$. Assume thermodynamic stability: for some $B\geq0$, $$\label{eq:pre-stability} \sum_{\substack{i,j\in R\\i<j}}\Phi(q_i-q_j)\geq-B\|R\|$$ for every finite configuration of distinct points. The potential is fixed independently of $\varepsilon$.
- **exact initial ensemble** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): Let $\mu=\varepsilon^{-2}$ and $${\cal D}_R^\varepsilon =\{Z_R:\|x_i-x_j\|>\varepsilon\text{ for all }i\ne j\in R\}.$$ For the prescribed $C^1$ nonnegative probability density $f_0$, set $$\begin{aligned} {\cal Z}_\varepsilon &=\sum_{n=0}^\infty\frac{\mu^n}{n!} \int f_0^{\otimes n}(Z_n) \mathbf1_{{\cal D}_{[n]}^\varepsilon}(Z_n)\,dZ_n, \label{eq:pre-partition}\\ W_{0,n}(Z_n) &={\cal Z}_\varepsilon^{-1}\mu^n f_0^{\otimes n}(Z_n) \mathbf1_{{\cal D}_{[n]}^\varepsilon}(Z_n). \label{eq:pre-ensemble} \end{aligned}$$ Here $[n]=\{1,\ldots,n\}$ and empty products equal one. The probability law on ordered coordinates is $$\label{pre:model} \mathbb P(N=n,Z_n\in dZ_n)=\frac1{n!}W_{0,n}(Z_n)\,dZ_n.$$ Thereafter the cloud evolves by <a href="#pre:dynamics" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:dynamics">[pre:dynamics]</a>.
- **polymer** ([source](#eq:pre-isolated)): A polymer is a connected component of the instantaneous graph with edges $\|x_i-x_j\|\leq\varepsilon$.
- **scattering collision operator** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-2)): Write $c=(v+v_*)/2$, $g=v-v_*$, and, for almost every incoming datum, let $g_{\rm out}$ be the velocity after exit. Set $$v'=c+\tfrac12g_{\rm out},\qquad v_*'=c-\tfrac12g_{\rm out}.$$ For $\psi\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^3)$, define $Q_\Phi$ at each $x$ by $$\label{pre:collision} \begin{split} \int Q_\Phi(f,f)(x,v)\psi(v)\,dv =\frac12\int_{\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3} & f(x,v)f(x,v_*)\|v-v_*\|\\ {}\times\int_{\substack{b\in(v-v_*)^\perp\\\|b\|<1}} &[\,\psi(v')+\psi(v_*')-\psi(v)-\psi(v_*)\,]\, d^2b\,dv\,dv_* . \end{split}$$ Here $d^2b$ is Euclidean area, and the $g=0$ contribution is zero. Null exceptional scattering data are ignored.
- **cell diagram** ([source](#stmt-def-cell-diagram)): For $R\subset S$, with $R\ne\varnothing$, a cell diagram on $(R,S)$ is $$\gamma=(A;\Gamma_1,\ldots,\Gamma_\ell).$$ Here $R\subset A\subset S$; the nonempty sets in all the $\Gamma_g$ together partition $S\setminus A$; and each $\Gamma_g$ is a nonempty unordered collection of these sets. Put $\Gamma_0=\{A\}$. Different members of the diagram are different force groups. Let $\iota_0(\gamma)$ be the indicator that every force group in the diagram is a union of blocks of $\pi_0(S)$. Equivalently, all pairs in different force groups have distance greater than $\varepsilon$ at $a$. Define $$\begin{aligned} w_I(\gamma;Z_S) ={}&\iota_0(\gamma)c_I^R(A) \prod_{g=1}^{\ell} \left[ \prod_{B\in\Gamma_g}c_I(B)\tau_I(\Gamma_{g-1},B) \prod_{\{B,C\}\subset\Gamma_g}\alpha_I(B,C) \right], \label{eq:cell-weight}\\ \sigma(\gamma)={}&(-1)^{\sum_{g=1}^{\ell}\|\Gamma_g\|}. \label{eq:cell-sign} \end{aligned}$$ Only the root group $A$ uses a joint flow containing roots. In particular, a contact between different generations is a condition on separate trajectories, not a force exerted between their groups. The empty list of generations is allowed when $S=A$.
- **whole-cell history** ([source](#stmt-cell-histories)): A finite whole-cell history is a composition of the diagram kernels above on successive cells, together with any tensor products and singleton-slot deletions or independent insertions specified by the exact centered identities at layer cuts. Its discrete prescription records the following data: 1. every formal label and its lifetime, with a fresh label for each independent input and no reuse of an independent source; 2. on each occupied cell, the required root labels $R$, the full set $S$, its root force group, and its detached groups and generations; 3. all the indicators in <a href="#eq:cell-weight" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cell-weight">[eq:cell-weight]</a> and the endpoint isolation indicators, including the initial polymer restrictions restored at every deleted singleton slot; 4. the signs, factorial weights, and any deterministic stopping marks and unexpanded factors. On an ordinary hierarchy cell, labels in $R$ persist through its upper endpoint; labels in $S\setminus R$ end their cell lifetime there. In the forward direction they were present throughout the cell, not created at a first-contact time. The root group is isolated from its departing members at the upper endpoint by $e_I^R(A)$. Detached groups have separate force systems; their geometric contacts with other generations at that endpoint are unrestricted. At the lower endpoint all force groups are unions of initial polymers. At a layer cut a nonsingleton polymer remains intact; independent singleton inputs are inserted only with the full polymer-pattern restrictions of the centered identity. The size $p$ is the number of distinct formal label lifetimes, not the number of their repeated appearances at cuts. All transport maps and indicators are defined on the full coordinates of these lifetimes. A history with a stopped factor can still depend on the unexpanded microscopic cloud through its last factor in $[0,1]$; its positive majorant drops that factor.
- **unisolated terminal witness block** ([source](#stmt-def-cell-terminal-witness)): A terminal witness is permitted a different upper boundary condition from an ordinary hierarchy cell. For $R\subset A$ put $$\mathsf J^I_{R,A}(Z_A;\,dY_R) =\delta_{(X_i^A(\delta),V_i^A(\delta))_{i\in R}}(\,dY_R), \label{eq:cell-J}$$ with no endpoint isolation factor. Its exact positive root-component decomposition is $$\mathsf J^I_{R,S} =\sum_{\substack{R\subset A\subset S\\ A\text{ union of blocks of }\pi_0(S)}} c_I^R(A)\mathsf J^I_{R,A}\Xi_I(\{A\};S\setminus A). \label{eq:cell-unisolated-root-decomposition}$$ Indeed the unique actual union of rooted contact components is still the unique nonzero summand, and its root trajectories agree with the full ones. This proves the identity without asserting endpoint isolation. Integrating a nonnegative test and dropping its last avoidance factor gives the corresponding positive bound. For a terminal witness block, all selected force groups end their formal lifetimes together at the fixed upper endpoint $b$. The distinguished labels $R$—usually a single label—serve only for factorial normalization and spatial integration anchors. They do not persist as a force subsystem after that endpoint. All terminal coordinates of every selected group are retained in the integration and energy bookkeeping before any projection in <a href="#eq:cell-J" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cell-J">[eq:cell-J]</a>. In particular one keeps the full terminal group Hamiltonians; one does not split $R$ away from close companions. Geometric isolation of $R$ from $A\setminus R$ at $b$ is neither required nor inferred. A selected block may also carry a measurable nonnegative trajectory indicator $W_I(A;Z_A)\leq1$ specifying the witness. Such an indicator is evaluated on its isolated group flow. If the witness was identified at an earlier time inside the cell, keep that prefix condition and continue the isolated group to the fixed endpoint $b$ before using whole-cell coordinates. This is a finite prescription with an extra indicator, not a stopping-time change of phase-volume variables. Establishing that an actual bad event admits a selected witness of this form belongs to [close:witnesses](#close:witnesses). At every earlier cut the ordinary rules of Definition <a href="#cell:histories" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cell:histories">17</a> remain in force. In particular, a selected union is initially externally isolated and its backward continuation across the preceding cell retains the usual endpoint isolation indicator. Only the final witness boundary uses <a href="#eq:cell-J" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cell-J">[eq:cell-J]</a>. These are formal operations in the witness integral; the microscopic evolution is not stopped or modified.
- **patternwise centered marginal** ([source](#stmt-cent-definition)): The centered marginal on pattern $\pi$ is $$\label{cent:definition-eq} E_\pi^\varepsilon(t,Z_R)=\chi_\pi^\varepsilon(Z_R) \sum_{A\subset S(\pi)}(-1)^{\|S(\pi)\setminus A\|} f_{S(\pi)\setminus A}(t,Z_{S(\pi)\setminus A}) \widetilde F_{B(\pi)\cup A}^\varepsilon(t,Z_{B(\pi)\cup A}).$$ We set $E_\varnothing^\varepsilon=1$. A nonsingleton block is always retained as a whole; its individual labels are not centered.
- **disjoint ownership** ([source](#stmt-cent-ownership)): Fix the labels, their order, and an original contact record for each occupied cell. At the top of a layer give each root label its own owner. Carry these owners backward through the cells. In a cell form the finite graph of physical and required virtual contacts of each original factor’s displayed generation diagram, including initial-polymer edges. Take the disjoint union over tensor factors; contacts with other factors remain tests and marks below, and are not used to assign original parents. The selected original pin edges, together with internal contact trees of its pinned groups, connect every new label to the old array. For an internal tree choose the lexicographically first spanning tree of the finite contact graph. Root the resulting forest at the old array, retaining the prescribed original parent in the singleton case of Section <a href="#cell:contact-records" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cell:contact-records">3.8</a>. More explicitly, process the selected edges in their record order, with the internal-tree and label orders resolving ties: keep an edge joining two distinct components when at least one is rootless, and omit an edge within one component or between two components that already contain old labels. Orient the retained trees toward their old-label endpoints. If needed, process the remaining required contact edges in the same fixed order to finish this rooted forest. Connectivity guarantees that no new label remains rootless. Every new label inherits the owner of its old-label endpoint; an old label never changes owner. An omitted edge between two already owned components is still a displayed contact. If its endpoint owners differ, mark both endpoint lives. Likewise mark both endpoints of every other contact between different owners, whether or not the record selected that contact as a pin. A multiple addition marks every new label and each old label participating in its pins or contacts. The *family* of a root is the set of labels with that root as owner. This definition changes neither a force group nor a trajectory, and its families are disjoint by construction.
- **logarithmic scale P** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Throughout the proof, $P=1+\|\log\varepsilon\|$;

All terminal coordinates of every selected group are retained in the integration and energy bookkeeping before any projection in <a href=\"#eq:cell-J\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cell-J\">[eq:cell-J]</a>. In particular one keeps the full terminal group Hamiltonians; one does not split $R$ away from close companions. Geometric isolation of $R$ from $A\\setminus R$ at $b$ is neither required nor inferred.\n\nA selected block may also carry a measurable nonnegative trajectory indicator $W_I(A;Z_A)\\leq1$ specifying the witness. Such an indicator is evaluated on its isolated group flow. If the witness was identified at an earlier time inside the cell, keep that prefix condition and continue the isolated group to the fixed endpoint $b$ before using whole-cell coordinates. This is a finite prescription with an extra indicator, not a stopping-time change of phase-volume variables. Establishing that an actual bad event admits a selected witness of this form belongs to [close:witnesses](#close:witnesses).\n\nAt every earlier cut the ordinary rules of Definition <a href=\"#cell:histories\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cell:histories\">17</a> remain in force. In particular, a selected union is initially externally isolated and its backward continuation across the preceding cell retains the usual endpoint isolation indicator. Only the final witness boundary uses <a href=\"#eq:cell-J\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cell-J\">[eq:cell-J]</a>. These are formal operations in the witness integral; the microscopic evolution is not stopped or modified.",
          "source_id": "PM-364.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "A terminal witness is permitted a different upper boundary condition from an ordinary hierarchy cell. For $R\\subset A$ put $$\\mathsf J^I_{R,A}(Z_A;\\,dY_R)\n   =\\delta_{(X_i^A(\\delta),V_i^A(\\delta))_{i\\in R}}(\\,dY_R),\n \\label{eq:cell-J}$$ with no endpoint isolation factor. Its exact positive root-component decomposition is $$\\mathsf J^I_{R,S}\n  =\\sum_{\\substack{R\\subset A\\subset S\\\\\n                   A\\text{ union of blocks of }\\pi_0(S)}}\n      c_I^R(A)\\mathsf J^I_{R,A}\\Xi_I(\\{A\\};S\\setminus A).\n \\label{eq:cell-unisolated-root-decomposition}$$ Indeed the unique actual union of rooted contact components is still the unique nonzero summand, and its root trajectories agree with the full ones. This proves the identity without asserting endpoint isolation. Integrating a nonnegative test and dropping its last avoidance factor gives the corresponding positive bound.\n\nFor a terminal witness block, all selected force groups end their formal lifetimes together at the fixed upper endpoint $b$. The distinguished labels $R$—usually a single label—serve only for factorial normalization and spatial integration anchors. They do not persist as a force subsystem after that endpoint. All terminal coordinates of every selected group are retained in the integration and energy bookkeeping before any projection in <a href=\"#eq:cell-J\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cell-J\">[eq:cell-J]</a>. In particular one keeps the full terminal group Hamiltonians; one does not split $R$ away from close companions. Geometric isolation of $R$ from $A\\setminus R$ at $b$ is neither required nor inferred.\n\nA selected block may also carry a measurable nonnegative trajectory indicator $W_I(A;Z_A)\\leq1$ specifying the witness. Such an indicator is evaluated on its isolated group flow. If the witness was identified at an earlier time inside the cell, keep that prefix condition and continue the isolated group to the fixed endpoint $b$ before using whole-cell coordinates. This is a finite prescription with an extra indicator, not a stopping-time change of phase-volume variables. Establishing that an actual bad event admits a selected witness of this form belongs to [close:witnesses](#close:witnesses).\n\nAt every earlier cut the ordinary rules of Definition <a href=\"#cell:histories\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cell:histories\">17</a> remain in force. In particular, a selected union is initially externally isolated and its backward continuation across the preceding cell retains the usual endpoint isolation indicator. Only the final witness boundary uses <a href=\"#eq:cell-J\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:cell-J\">[eq:cell-J]</a>. These are formal operations in the witness integral; the microscopic evolution is not stopped or modified.",

          "exact_quote": "Fix the labels, their order, and an original contact record for each occupied cell. At the top of a layer give each root label its own owner. Carry these owners backward through the cells. In a cell form the finite graph of physical and required virtual contacts of each original factor’s displayed generation diagram, including initial-polymer edges. Take the disjoint union over tensor factors; contacts with other factors remain tests and marks below, and are not used to assign original parents. The selected original pin edges, together with internal contact trees of its pinned groups, connect every new label to the old array. For an internal tree choose the lexicographically first spanning tree of the finite contact graph. Root the resulting forest at the old array, retaining the prescribed original parent in the singleton case of Section <a href=\"#cell:contact-records\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cell:contact-records\">3.8</a>. More explicitly, process the selected edges in their record order, with the internal-tree and label orders resolving ties: keep an edge joining two distinct components when at least one is rootless, and omit an edge within one component or between two components that already contain old labels. Orient the retained trees toward their old-label endpoints. If needed, process the remaining required contact edges in the same fixed order to finish this rooted forest. Connectivity guarantees that no new label remains rootless.\n\nEvery new label inherits the owner of its old-label endpoint; an old label never changes owner. An omitted edge between two already owned components is still a displayed contact. If its endpoint owners differ, mark both endpoint lives. Likewise mark both endpoints of every other contact between different owners, whether or not the record selected that contact as a pin. A multiple addition marks every new label and each old label participating in its pins or contacts. The *family* of a root is the set of labels with that root as owner. This definition changes neither a force group nor a trajectory, and its families are disjoint by construction.",

        }

| q-stopping-cutoff | layer stopping cutoff | = | $\lceil P^3U_j\rceil$ | each layer index |
| q-witness-probability | probability of an oversized ancestral cluster | ≤ | $e^{-8D_j}$ | sufficiently small epsilon |
| q-layer-remainder | layer remainder norm | ≤ | $e^{-8K_j}$ | sufficiently small epsilon |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-flow** [claim](#stmt-pre-flow) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-flow): Local Lipschitz solution, energy conservation and stability bound, zero divergence -> global volume-preserving flow
- **step-initial** [claim](#stmt-pre-initial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-initial): Insertion formula, maximal separated subcollection and factorial bound -> initial centered smallness
- **step-duration** [claim](#stmt-pre-duration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-duration): Flow-box change of variables and finite volume of the image -> residence-time flux bound
- **step-operator** [claim](#stmt-pre-operator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-operator): Scattering involution preserves flux -> strong form of the collision operator
- **step-scattering** [claim](#stmt-pre-scattering) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-scattering): Sard theorem on effective radial potential and strict convexity of the angular sweep -> nondegenerate scattering outside null set
- **step-envelopes** [claim](#stmt-pre-envelopes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-envelopes): Volterra iteration of a positive damped operator -> summed Gaussian envelopes
- **step-cell-expansion** [claim](#stmt-cell-expansion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cell-expansion): Root decomposition, generation recursion and Boolean inversion -> exact cell expansion
- **step-cell-factorial** [claim](#stmt-cor-cell-factorial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-cell-factorial): Apply expansion to each root tuple and group coefficients -> factorial identity across a cell
- **step-cell-factorization** [claim](#stmt-cell-factorization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cell-factorization): Cross avoidance splits flows and indicators -> diagramwise tensor factorization
- **step-cell-stopping** [claim](#stmt-cell-stopping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cell-stopping): Weighted product identity with stopping at a threshold -> stopped expansion with remainder
- **step-cent-identity** [claim](#stmt-cent-identity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cent-identity): Binomial cancellation -> exact inversion
- **step-cent-collapse** [claim](#stmt-cent-pattern-collapse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cent-pattern-collapse): One pattern per array, collapse of the pattern sum -> norm bounds for the transforms
- **step-cent-residual** [claim](#stmt-cent-residual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cent-residual): Tree summability, null-set exclusion of meetings, dominated convergence -> short-layer residual vanishes
- **step-cent-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-cent-cancellation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cent-cancellation): Involution on the root tag using factorization -> cancellation of simple families
- **step-cent-charges** [claim](#stmt-cent-charges) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cent-charges): Downward path decomposition of charged roots -> extensive charge count
- **step-hist-gaussian** [claim](#stmt-hist-gaussian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hist-gaussian): Volume preservation and energy ledger -> once-counted Gaussian
- **step-hist-pinning** [claim](#stmt-hist-pinning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hist-pinning): Triangular first-contact translation charts -> pin Jacobians and counts
- **step-hist-basic** [claim](#stmt-hist-basic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hist-basic): Parent sums, ordered time simplex and shell summation -> finite-term bound with mesh gain
- **step-hist-irregular** [claim](#stmt-hist-irregular) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hist-irregular): Adapted conditional probabilities and Cauchy-Schwarz -> exponential suppression of marked additions
- **step-hist-pivot** [claim](#stmt-hist-pivot) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hist-pivot): Two-sided earliest-contact scan from a pivot time -> proximity volume bound
- **step-hist-crowding** [claim](#stmt-hist-crowding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hist-crowding): Forest from proximity graph on a time grid -> crowding suppression
- **step-hist-separated** [claim](#stmt-hist-separated) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hist-separated): Uniform parent selection against a crowding gate -> separated original parents
- **step-exp-rerouting** [claim](#stmt-exp-rerouting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-rerouting): Adapted tube estimate in original coordinates -> premature contact suppression
- **step-exp-tube** [claim](#stmt-exp-tube) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-tube): Mesh covering of a spherical cap and dyadic summation -> angular tube bound
- **step-exp-terminal** [claim](#stmt-exp-terminal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-terminal): Hook-length count of increasing trees and greedy antichain selection -> disjoint regular terminal trees
- **step-exp-exposure** [claim](#stmt-exp-exposure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-exposure): Chronological replay of hidden blocks with product-measure domination -> geometric factor per selected tree
- **step-exp-defects** [claim](#stmt-exp-defects) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-defects): Combine exclusions with the exposure bound -> extensive defect gain
- **step-exp-finite** [claim](#stmt-exp-finite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-finite): Spatial tube estimate for fixed number of labels -> vanishing fixed-size defects
- **step-close-entropy** [claim](#stmt-close-entropy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-close-entropy): Coefficient extraction in a binomial product -> retained-slot entropy bound
- **step-close-energy** [claim](#stmt-close-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-close-energy): Energy shells and arithmetic-geometric mean -> small per-label base
- **step-close-summability** [claim](#stmt-close-summability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-close-summability): Split few-crossing and many-crossing histories -> exponential summability
- **step-close-witness** [claim](#stmt-close-witness-extraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-close-witness-extraction): Trace ancestries backward to a bounded positive witness -> bounded witness history
- **step-close-amplification** [claim](#stmt-close-amplification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-close-amplification): Total variation bound of a finite block and moment bound -> amplification and bad-cloud discard
- **step-close-tails** [claim](#stmt-close-tails) / [proof](#proof-stmt-close-tails): Induction over layers -> exponential tail bounds
- **step-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-close-tails-2): Expand centered arrays, remove remainders, use mass conservation and Chebyshev -> fixed-order chaos and empirical convergence

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.roadmap.v2 -->

        {

          "exact_quote": "by the one-dimensional Sard theorem on a countable compact exhaustion.",
          "source_id": "PM-364.1"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "Exceptional scattering sets are shown null by Sard theorem and countable exhaustion, giving only null-set statements and no computable rate.",
      "step_id": "step-scattering"
    }
  ],
  "pm_id": "PM-364.1",

  "steps": [
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-pre-flow",

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-pre-flow",
          "exact_quote": "For each fixed $\\varepsilon>0$ and finite label set $R$, <a href=\"#pre:dynamics\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"pre:dynamics\">[pre:dynamics]</a> has a unique global flow from every finite-energy initial state in its domain.",
          "source_id": "PM-364.1"

      "inputs": [

      ],

    {

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-pre-duration",
          "exact_quote": "For every $M<\\infty$ there is $C_M<\\infty$ such that $$\\label{eq:pre-duration}\n \\int_{\\substack{|g|\\leq M\\\\n\\in S^2,\\ n\\cdot g<0}}\n \\tau_*(n,g)|n\\cdot g|\\,d\\sigma(n)\\,dg\\leq C_M.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-364.1"
        }
      ],

        }

        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-cell-expansion",
          "exact_quote": "Let $R\\subset S$ and let $A_0$ be the union of the blocks of $\\pi_0(S)$ meeting $R$.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-cell-factorial",
          "exact_quote": "For the actual microscopic cloud and $r=|R|$, the cell identity is written after relabelling the ordered roots as $R=\\{1,\\ldots,r\\}$: $$\\widetilde F_R(b)\n  =\\sum_{m\\geq0}\\frac{\\mu^m}{m!}\n       \\mathcal T^I_{R,R\\sqcup\\{r+1,\\ldots,r+m\\}}\n       \\widetilde F_{r+m}(a).\n \\label{eq:cell-factorial}$$ The right side is interpreted with each finite Boolean coefficient grouped before summing over $m$.",
          "source_id": "PM-364.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-cell-factorial",
      "inputs": [

      ],

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-factorial-chaos**: On a time interval where the classical kinetic solution exists with a uniform Gaussian bound, every fixed-order rescaled factorial marginal of the Newtonian gas converges in L1 to the tensor power of the kinetic solution, uniformly in time.
- **inv-potential-class**: The result is posed only for radial pair potentials that vanish outside the unit ball, are twice continuously differentiable away from an optional repulsive singularity at the origin, and satisfy thermodynamic stability.
- **inv-initial-centering**: At time zero, the centered correlation of the externally isolated factorial densities is bounded by a small geometric factor per retained label times the product initial density.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-long-range-forces**: Applying the kinetic derivation to unshielded long-range interactions such as Coulomb or gravitational forces. -> The potential class has compact support, so the whole expansion and the two-body flux analysis do not cover long-range forces.
- **anti-beyond-lifespan**: Using the convergence statement at times beyond the interval on which the classical solution is regular and Gaussian bounded. -> The theorem takes the regular classical solution as an input on [0,T] and gives no guarantee past it.
- **anti-manybody-two-body**: Reading the residence-time flux lemma as control of many-particle bound clusters. -> The lemma concerns isolated two-body encounters only; cluster behaviour needs the separate history estimates.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The paper proves an asymptotic limit theorem as the exclusion distance tends to zero for a stochastic Newtonian gas, using non-explicit compactness and null-set exclusions. It states no finite procedure that outputs a computed object, and its rate is not made explicit, so no reference implementation with an independent oracle can decide the stated claims. The finite diagram identities are bookkeeping steps inside the proof of that limit.

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |

| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves a limit theorem for a microscopic Newtonian gas. It states no procedure with explicit steps that could be implemented as a library or program. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result gives mathematical background for replacing many-body forces by two-body scattering in rarefied-gas particle simulations, but it contains no simulation algorithm. At most it is inspirational for a collision-kernel choice. Its hypotheses (finite-range radial potential, regular kinetic interval) bound where that justification is valid. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool, checker or certificate procedure is produced. The statements are existence and limit assertions with non-explicit constants. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proofs are analytic and combinatorial bounds on finite history integrals. They define no algorithm with inputs, outputs and a complexity. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Contact graphs, forests and cell diagrams appear only as bookkeeping inside proofs. No data structure with operations and costs is proposed. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns arithmetic kernels, parallel execution or hardware mapping. The statements are asymptotic in epsilon. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no hardness assumption, adversary or protocol. The paper is classical kinetic theory. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No statistical learning, training objective or data model appears. Probabilistic statements concern the initial grand-canonical gas law. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, constraint set or optimality notion occurs. Thermodynamic stability is a hypothesis on the potential, not an optimisation problem. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No communication, consensus or fault model is present. Particles interact through a single global Newtonian flow. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The natural home of the result is mathematical physics: rigorous derivation of the nonlinear Boltzmann equation. It supports no engineering contract of its own and bounds only empirical observables of the first two marginals. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **hyp-finite-range-radial** (hypothesis): The theorem is posed only for radial pair potentials that vanish at distance one and beyond, are twice continuously differentiable except for an optional repulsive singularity at the origin, and are fixed independently of the exclusion distance. Blocked use: ['Applying the kinetic limit to long-range forces such as unshielded Coulomb or gravitational interactions', 'Applying the limit to anisotropic or non-radial molecular potentials', 'Letting the potential depend on the exclusion distance']
- **hyp-thermodynamic-stability** (hypothesis): The only sign condition on the potential is thermodynamic stability with some constant B at least zero; this stability bound drives the energy ledger, the global flow and the Gaussian budget of the finite-history estimates. Blocked use: ['Using the result for potentials that violate the stability inequality, for example collapsing attractive interactions', 'Reusing the energy and Gaussian estimates for unstable many-body systems']
- **hyp-regular-kinetic-lifespan** (hypothesis): The conclusion holds only on a finite interval on which the classical kinetic equation has a nonnegative classical probability-density solution with a uniform Gaussian velocity bound, starting from C^1 data with summable Gaussian bounds on the density and its spatial gradient; this solution is an input, not a consequence. Blocked use: ['Using the convergence statement at times beyond the regular interval or across shocks and singularities', 'Using the theorem to prove existence or regularity of the kinetic solution', 'Applying it to initial data without the summed Gaussian bounds']
- **hyp-bounded-initial-density** (hypothesis): The initial centered-correlation bound and the mass-defect estimate assume that the spatial density of the initial profile is bounded. Blocked use: ['Using the small per-label centered correlation for initial data with unbounded spatial density']
- **edge-nonsingular-coincidence** (edge_case): In the nonsingular potential case coincident positions are permitted, whereas in the singular case pair distances stay bounded away from zero along each trajectory; the two cases are handled separately in the flow and chart arguments. Blocked use: ['Assuming a positive minimum pair distance in the nonsingular case', 'Assuming strictly distinct positions when transferring flow statements to bounded potentials']
- **nc-scattering-null-sets** (nonconstructive): The nondegeneracy of scattering holds only outside a set of zero incoming flux and is obtained through Sard-type and countable-exhaustion arguments; no explicit exceptional set or rate is provided, and the two outgoing increments need not be independent. Blocked use: ['Extracting a computable bound or explicit exceptional parameter set from the scattering analysis', 'Treating the two outgoing velocity increments as independent random variables']
- **scope-two-body-flux** (formal_scope): The residence-time flux integrability concerns isolated two-body encounters only; many-particle bound components and conditional parameter statements for dynamically formed clusters are treated separately by the history estimates. Blocked use: ['Reading the residence-time lemma as control of many-particle clusters', 'Using the rotational marginals as independence from a trajectory that depends on the same parameters']
- **scope-empirical-corollary** (formal_scope): The empirical-observable statement is a convergence in probability for compactly supported smooth tests at deterministic observation times, derived from the first two marginals; it is not a statement about fluctuations or about suprema inside a sample path. Blocked use: ['Using it for central-limit or fluctuation-scale questions', 'Using it for path-wise suprema over time', 'Using it for observables that are not smooth and compactly supported']
- **scope-cutoff-mismatch-uncancelled** (formal_scope): The exact cancellation of simple families applies only to the retained operator below the size cutoff; the finite cutoff-mismatch operator stays in the recurrence with no cancellation and no positive physical remainder bound asserted for it. Blocked use: ['Assuming that all simple-family terms cancel at every formal size', 'Bounding the mismatch operator by positive stopped-cloud domination']
- **unknown-explicit-rate** (unknown): No explicit convergence rate in the exclusion distance and no explicit constants are supplied; the cutoff schedule is polynomial in the logarithmic scale and the size thresholds are non-explicit. Blocked use: ['Deriving step sizes, particle counts or error tolerances for a numerical scheme from the theorem', 'Quoting an algebraic rate of convergence']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We derive the nonlinear Boltzmann equation from a grand-canonical Newtonian gas with initial pair exclusion. We treat stable, finite-range radial potentials that are <i>C</i><sup>2</sup> except for an allowed repulsive singularity at the origin, and <i>C</i><sup>1</sup> initial probability densities with spatially summable Gaussian bounds on the density and its spatial gradient. All fixed-order rescaled factorial marginals converge in <i>L</i><sup>1</sup>, uniformly throughout every finite interval on which the classical kinetic solution has a uniform Gaussian bound. The result allows attractive wells and dynamically formed clusters, without restrictions on the differential scattering cross-section.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The Boltzmann–Grad limit describes a gas whose interaction range tends to zero while the collision frequency remains of order one. In three dimensions the activity is $\mu_\varepsilon=\varepsilon^{-2}$. The microscopic dynamics is Newtonian, whereas the limiting collision operator is determined by the actual two-body scattering of the pair potential. We prove this limit for stable finite-range radial potentials on every finite interval where the Boltzmann solution has the regularity and Gaussian bound specified below.

<a id="intro:norm"></a>
Write $z=(x,v)\in\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3$. For a phase-space function $h$ and $\gamma>0$, write $$\label{intro:norm}
 \|h\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\gamma}
 :=\sum_{k\in\mathbb Z^3}\;
 \sup_{\substack{|x-k|\le1\\v\in\mathbb R^3}}
 e^{\gamma|v|^2}|h(x,v)|.$$ For a vector-valued function we use its Euclidean length inside this definition. The precise potential class, grand-canonical law, Newton equations, and scattering operator are given in Assumption <a href="#pre:potential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:potential">1</a> and Equations <a href="#pre:model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:model">[pre:model]</a>, <a href="#pre:dynamics" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:dynamics">[pre:dynamics]</a>, and <a href="#pre:collision" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:collision">[pre:collision]</a>. In brief, the potential is radial, vanishes beyond unit distance, and is $C^2$, with either a finite value or a repulsive singularity at the origin. Its only sign condition is thermodynamic stability, in the classical sense of (Ruelle 1969) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Ruelle69](#ref-Ruelle69)]. The initial law is the product activity $f_0$ conditioned by pairwise exclusion at distance $\varepsilon$, with its exact grand-canonical normalization. For each fixed integer $s\ge1$, the scaled factorial density $F_s^\varepsilon(t)$ is characterized by <a id="intro:factorial-test"></a>$$\label{intro:factorial-test}
 \int a(Z_s)F_s^\varepsilon(t,Z_s)\,dZ_s
 =\mu_\varepsilon^{-s}\mathbb E
   \sum_{\substack{1\le i_1,\ldots,i_s\le N\\
                    i_1,\ldots,i_s\ \mathrm{distinct}}}
   a(z_{i_1}(t),\ldots,z_{i_s}(t))$$ for bounded measurable tests $a$ on $(\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3)^s$. The sum runs over ordered distinct labels and is zero when $N<s$; $Z_s=(z_1,\ldots,z_s)$ denotes an ordered phase-space configuration.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="maintheorem">

<a id="intro:data-bound"></a>
<a id="intro:solution-bound"></a>
<a id="intro:chaos"></a>
**Theorem 1** (Regular-lifespan kinetic limit). *Let $\Phi$ satisfy Assumption <a href="#pre:potential" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:potential">1</a>, and let $\beta>0$. Let $f_0\ge0$ be a $C^1$ probability density on $\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3$ such that $$\label{intro:data-bound}
 \|f_0\|_{\mathrm{Bol},2\beta}
 +\|\nabla_xf_0\|_{\mathrm{Bol},2\beta}<\infty.$$ Fix $0<T<\infty$ for which the equation <a id="intro:boltzmann"></a>$$\label{intro:boltzmann}
 (\partial_t+v\cdot\nabla_x)f=Q_\Phi(f,f),
 \qquad f|_{t=0}=f_0,$$ has a nonnegative classical probability-density solution satisfying $$\label{intro:solution-bound}
 \sup_{0\le t\le T}\sup_{x,v}
 e^{2\beta|v|^2}f(t,x,v)<\infty.$$ Start the exact Newtonian process from the grand-canonical law <a href="#pre:model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:model">[pre:model]</a>. For its scaled factorial densities $F_s^\varepsilon$, defined in <a href="#pre:factorial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:factorial">[pre:factorial]</a>, every fixed integer $s\ge1$ satisfies $$\label{intro:chaos}
 \lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}\;
 \sup_{0\le t\le T}
 \bigl\|F_s^\varepsilon(t)-f(t)^{\otimes s}\bigr\|_
 {L^1((\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3)^s)}=0.$$*

</div>

The potential and $T$ are fixed independently of $\varepsilon$. The convergence modulus may depend on the potential and on the bounds in <a href="#intro:data-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:data-bound">[intro:data-bound]</a>–<a href="#intro:solution-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:solution-bound">[intro:solution-bound]</a>. Attractive wells, long scattering delays, and noninjective scattering maps are permitted. The exclusion belongs solely to the initial law. The Newtonian evolution retains simultaneous interactions, recollisions, and any clusters it creates.

<a id="stmt-cor-empirical"></a>
<div id="cor:empirical" class="maincorollary">

<a id="intro:empirical"></a>
**Corollary 2** (Empirical observables). *Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, let $a\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3;\mathbb R)$ and $\eta>0$. Then $$\label{intro:empirical}
 \lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}\;
 \sup_{0\le t\le T}
 \mathbb P\!\left(
 \left|\mu_\varepsilon^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}a(z_i(t))
       -\int a(z)f(t,z)\,dz\right|>\eta\right)=0.$$*

</div>

The supremum in <a href="#intro:empirical" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:empirical">[intro:empirical]</a> is over deterministic observation times. The conclusion follows from the first two factorial marginals and conservation of particle number; see Section <a href="#sec:closure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:closure">7</a>.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and scope

The Boltzmann–Grad scaling goes back to Grad (Grad 1949, 1958) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Grad49](#ref-Grad49), [Grad58](#ref-Grad58)]. Lanford established the hard-sphere limit on a short time interval (Lanford 1975) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Lanford75](#ref-Lanford75)], and King’s thesis treated positive finite-range potentials (King 1975) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[King75](#ref-King75)]. Gallagher, Saint-Raymond, and Texier gave a detailed geometric analysis of recollisions for hard spheres and a class of repulsive potentials, including a convergence rate in the hard-sphere case (Gallagher, Saint-Raymond, and Texier 2014) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[GST14](#ref-GST14)]. Pulvirenti, Saffirio, and Simonella proved a short-time theorem for the stable radial class used here; see their Section 8, Hypothesis 1$'$ and Theorem 1$'$ (Pulvirenti, Saffirio, and Simonella 2014). Their treatment permits attractive parts and does not require a globally single-valued differential cross-section.

The time restriction in a collision-tree expansion reflects the growth of correlated histories. Global-time results were obtained for sufficiently rare, spatially dispersive gases in vacuum by Illner and Pulvirenti (Illner and Pulvirenti 1986, 1989; Pulvirenti 1987) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[IllnerPulvirenti86](#ref-IllnerPulvirenti86), [Pulvirenti87](#ref-Pulvirenti87), [IllnerPulvirenti89](#ref-IllnerPulvirenti89)]; the last reference corrects and improves the earlier results. For general data, extending a short-time limit requires control of the correlations already present at the next starting time. Pulvirenti and Simonella quantified the correlation errors remaining after independent factors are removed (Pulvirenti and Simonella 2017) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[PulvirentiSimonella2017](#ref-PulvirentiSimonella2017)]. Bodineau, Gallagher, Saint-Raymond, and Simonella developed a cluster expansion directly on physical trajectories (Bodineau et al. 2022) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[BGSSCluster2022](#ref-BGSSCluster2022)], distinguishing genuine collisions from geometric overlaps between independently evolved components.

Deng, Hani, and Ma proved the hard-sphere limit throughout every regular finite kinetic interval (Deng, Hani, and Ma 2025, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[DHM25](#ref-DHM25)]. Their layered expansion retains the earlier histories of correlated remainders while extracting the factors approximated by the kinetic solution. This strategy has an antecedent in the long-time wave-kinetic work of Deng and Hani (Deng and Hani 2024) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[DengHani2024](#ref-DengHani2024)]. The exposition (Bodineau et al. 2026) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[BGSS26](#ref-BGSS26)] explains both the accumulated correlations and the loss of topology that prevent restarting a short-time chaos theorem as a black box.

Smooth potentials require additional control because encounters last for positive time and several particles can interact simultaneously. Deng, Hani, and Ma discuss this extension as a further problem in (Deng, Hani, and Ma 2025, sec. 1.4.2) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[DHM25](#ref-DHM25)]. The present whole-component expansion retains the potential energy of these interactions. Its stability and causal exposure estimates are proved below; they are not supplied by the hard-sphere convergence theorem. Similarly, the assumed classical kinetic solution is an input to the theorem, while its additional spatial envelopes are derived in Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a> by adapting the argument of (Deng, Hani, and Ma 2025, Proposition A.1) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[DHM25](#ref-DHM25)].

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> proves the regular-lifespan Boltzmann–Grad statement for the full stable radial class. Its lifespan assumption concerns the kinetic solution itself and imposes no small-data or near-equilibrium condition. The conclusion is convergence of all fixed factorial marginals in full phase-space $L^1$.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Structure of the proof

The argument uses a finite number of macroscopic layers of length $b=T/L$, each divided into a much finer mesh. The fine cells permit an exact finite expansion by whole interaction components. The coarse layers permit centering against the known Boltzmann solution. Several features of the construction are useful beyond ordinary binary collision trees. Throughout the proof, $P=1+|\log\varepsilon|$; polynomial cutoffs mean fixed powers of $P$, chosen after the finite number $L$ of layers.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### Exact component identities.

The expansion groups particles into their entire instantaneous interaction components at every cut. A finite inclusion-exclusion over successive generations of components represents each coefficient by independent subsystem flows and contact indicators. This preserves the potential energy needed for estimates even when a component has many internal encounters. Centering acts only on isolated singleton slots. Its cancellation requires separation of every displayed trajectory, including the detached trajectories in the signed terms.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### One energy budget across all layers.

Each formal particle lifetime contributes an independent input once and departs once. Stability applied to intact departed components gives a single Gaussian budget for the whole history. Repeatedly carried velocities can then cost $L^{p/2}$ for a history with $p$ labels, while the time factors contribute $(T/L)^{p-h_0}$ for $h_0$ top roots. The $h_0$ roots are the labels at the upper end from which the backward history starts. After bounding their contribution, the resulting base $CT/\sqrt L$ is small for a sufficiently large fixed $L$. This is the mechanism that passes beyond the convergence interval of one collision-tree series.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### Causal exposure of defects.

The first estimates integrate all positions and velocities at the ends of a fine time cell. They bound histories with crowded groups or several additions in one cell. The remaining histories can be represented by trees whose edges follow the order in which particles are added. If extra contacts affect a positive fraction of the particle lives, a counting argument selects many disjoint trees of bounded size. Their fresh scattering parameters give small angular sets for contacts with paths that are independent of those parameters. To multiply these small factors, both outputs of an unexposed genuine scattering must remain hidden, whereas a virtual crossing hides only the new branch; Figure <a href="#fig:exposure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:exposure">1</a> explains the distinction. The trees used to choose integration coordinates need not coincide with the families used in the signed cancellation identity.

<figure id="fig:exposure">

<figcaption>The auxiliary exposure used to multiply contact savings. Blue branches carry an unrevealed launch parameter. A genuine scattering changes the continued parent’s velocity, so both descendant branches must be hidden. Passage leaves the parent unchanged. Until first exposure, the black trajectories can be computed without the hidden parameter. The construction is applied to exact signed histories; no particles are deleted from the microscopic dynamics. The drawing shows one selected tree. The full construction also omits added groups whose positions have not yet been fixed relative to the visible particles, including groups not selected for exposure; Proposition <a href="#exp:exposure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="exp:exposure">39</a> gives the complete construction.</figcaption>
</figure>

Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a> establishes the flow, initial estimates, scattering facts, and uniform smooth-input bounds. Section <a href="#sec:cell-expansion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cell-expansion">[sec:cell-expansion]</a> constructs the exact finite expansion. Section <a href="#sec:centering" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:centering">4</a> gives the centered identities and the short-layer approximation to the kinetic solution. Sections <a href="#sec:history-estimates" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:history-estimates">[sec:history-estimates]</a> and <a href="#sec:exposure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:exposure">[sec:exposure]</a> prove the finite-history measure estimates and the exposure bound. Section <a href="#sec:closure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:closure">7</a> chooses the thresholds, removes every cutoff, and proves Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> and Corollary <a href="#cor:empirical" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:empirical">2</a>.

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Microscopic flow, scattering, and kinetic envelopes

The microscopic expansion uses globally defined finite-particle flows and small initial centered correlations. We establish these facts, identify the collision operator by two-body flux, and derive fixed spatially summable Gaussian bounds for the assumed kinetic solution and its spatial gradient. The scattering arguments require neither a monotone potential nor a regular differential cross-section.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Potential assumptions and finite-particle flow

<a id="stmt-pre-potential"></a>
<div id="pre:potential" class="assumption">

<a id="eq:pre-stability"></a>
**Assumption 1** (Potential class). Write $\Phi(q)=\phi(|q|)$, with $\Phi(q)=0$ for $|q|\geq1$. Assume either $\Phi\in C^2(\mathbb R^3;\mathbb R)$, or $\Phi\in C^2(\mathbb R^3\setminus\{0\})$ with $\Phi(q)\to+\infty$ as $q\to0$. In the latter case set $\Phi(0)=+\infty$. Assume thermodynamic stability: for some $B\geq0$, $$\label{eq:pre-stability}
 \sum_{\substack{i,j\in R\\i<j}}\Phi(q_i-q_j)\geq-B|R|$$ for every finite configuration of distinct points. The potential is fixed independently of $\varepsilon$.

</div>

<a id="pre:dynamics"></a>
These are the assumptions of Hypothesis $1'$ in (Pulvirenti, Saffirio, and Simonella 2014, sec. 8) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[PSS14](#ref-PSS14)]. Set $$\Phi_\varepsilon(x)=\Phi(x/\varepsilon),\qquad
 \mathcal H_R(Z_R)=\frac12\sum_{i\in R}|v_i|^2+
       \sum_{\substack{i,j\in R\\i<j}}\Phi_\varepsilon(x_i-x_j).$$ The exact microscopic equations are $$\label{pre:dynamics}
 \dot x_i=v_i,\qquad
 \dot v_i=-\frac1\varepsilon\sum_{j\in R\setminus\{i\}}
             \nabla\Phi((x_i-x_j)/\varepsilon).$$

<a id="stmt-pre-flow"></a>
<div id="pre:flow" class="proposition">

**Proposition 2** (Global finite-particle flow). *For each fixed $\varepsilon>0$ and finite label set $R$, <a href="#pre:dynamics" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:dynamics">[pre:dynamics]</a> has a unique global flow from every finite-energy initial state in its domain. The flow preserves phase volume and $\mathcal H_R$. In the singular case, every pair distance along a given trajectory is bounded away from zero. In the nonsingular case coincident positions are permitted.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pre-flow"><summary>Proof of stmt-pre-flow</summary>

<a id="eq:pre-speed"></a>
*Proof.* Taking two particles in <a href="#eq:pre-stability" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre-stability">[eq:pre-stability]</a> gives $\phi(r)\geq-2B$ for $r>0$. In the nonsingular case, approximation by distinct configurations extends stability to coincident positions. The vector field is locally Lipschitz on its domain and hence has a unique maximal local solution. Differentiation gives energy conservation on its existence interval. For $n=|R|$, $$\label{eq:pre-speed}
 \frac12\sum_{i\in R}|v_i(t)|^2
       \leq\mathcal H_R(Z_R(0))+Bn.$$ For $n\geq2$, bounding every pair except one below gives <a id="eq:pre-pairupper"></a>$$\label{eq:pre-pairupper}
 \Phi_\varepsilon(x_i(t)-x_j(t))
 \leq\mathcal H_R(Z_R(0))
             +2B\left(\binom n2-1\right).$$ In the singular case this bounds every pair distance away from zero. Equation <a href="#eq:pre-speed" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre-speed">[eq:pre-speed]</a> prevents escape to spatial infinity in finite time. Thus on each bounded time interval the solution remains in a compact subset of the vector field’s domain and extends beyond any proposed finite endpoint, forward or backward. In the nonsingular case one can also use the globally Lipschitz gradient of the compactly supported $C^2$ potential directly. The cases $n=0,1$ are immediate.

The vector field has zero divergence in $(x_i,v_i)_{i\in R}$: $\dot x_i$ is independent of $x_i$ and $\dot v_i$ is independent of $v_i$. Its flow Jacobian therefore solves $\dot J=0$ with $J(0)=1$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### The ensemble and external isolation

<a id="stmt-auto-definition-1"></a>
<div class="definition">

<a id="pre:model"></a>
**Definition 3** (Exact initial ensemble). Let $\mu=\varepsilon^{-2}$ and $${\cal D}_R^\varepsilon
 =\{Z_R:|x_i-x_j|>\varepsilon\text{ for all }i\ne j\in R\}.$$ For the prescribed $C^1$ nonnegative probability density $f_0$, set <a id="eq:pre-partition"></a><a id="eq:pre-ensemble"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 {\cal Z}_\varepsilon
 &=\sum_{n=0}^\infty\frac{\mu^n}{n!}
   \int f_0^{\otimes n}(Z_n)
             \mathbf1_{{\cal D}_{[n]}^\varepsilon}(Z_n)\,dZ_n,
       \label{eq:pre-partition}\\
 W_{0,n}(Z_n)
 &={\cal Z}_\varepsilon^{-1}\mu^n f_0^{\otimes n}(Z_n)
             \mathbf1_{{\cal D}_{[n]}^\varepsilon}(Z_n).
       \label{eq:pre-ensemble}
\end{aligned}$$ Here $[n]=\{1,\ldots,n\}$ and empty products equal one. The probability law on ordered coordinates is $$\label{pre:model}
 \mathbb P(N=n,Z_n\in dZ_n)=\frac1{n!}W_{0,n}(Z_n)\,dZ_n.$$ Thereafter the cloud evolves by <a href="#pre:dynamics" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:dynamics">[pre:dynamics]</a>.

</div>

Since $1\leq\mathcal Z_\varepsilon\leq e^\mu$, this is a probability law with finite $N$ almost surely. Initial exclusion gives zero potential energy and finite kinetic energy. Proposition <a href="#pre:flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:flow">2</a> therefore defines its exact evolution. Let $W_{t,n}$ be the transported density.

<a id="pre:factorial"></a>
For $r=|R|$, the rescaled factorial density is $$\label{pre:factorial}
 F_R^\varepsilon(t,Z_R)=
 \mu^{-r}\sum_{n=0}^\infty\frac1{n!}
       \int W_{t,r+n}(Z_R,Z_{[n]}^{\rm ext})\,dZ_{[n]}^{\rm ext}.$$ External labels are distinct from those in $R$. Symmetry makes the formula independent of their temporary names. It equals the density defined by the ordered distinct-label sum in the statement: in a cloud of size $r+n$, there are $(r+n)!/n!$ such selections.

<a id="eq:pre-isolated"></a>
The externally isolated factorial density is $$\label{eq:pre-isolated}
 \widetilde F_R^\varepsilon(t,Z_R)=
 \mu^{-r}\sum_{n=0}^\infty\frac1{n!}
 \int W_{t,r+n}(Z_R,Z_{[n]}^{\rm ext})
 \prod_{\substack{i\in R\\j\in[n]^{\rm ext}}}
       \mathbf1_{\{|x_i-x_j|>\varepsilon\}}\,
             dZ_{[n]}^{\rm ext}.$$ Set $F_\varnothing^\varepsilon=\widetilde F_\varnothing^\varepsilon=1$. External isolation imposes no condition on distances within $R$. In particular an interacting displayed component can be externally isolated, and $0\leq\widetilde F_R^\varepsilon\leq F_R^\varepsilon$. A polymer is a connected component of the instantaneous graph with edges $|x_i-x_j|\leq\varepsilon$. The condition in <a href="#eq:pre-isolated" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre-isolated">[eq:pre-isolated]</a> says exactly that the displayed labels form a union of entire polymers of the full cloud.

<a id="stmt-pre-initial"></a>
<div id="pre:initial" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:pre-insertion"></a>
**Proposition 4** (Initial insertion and centering). *Put $\rho_0(x)=\int f_0(x,v)\,dv$, and assume $\|\rho_0\|_\infty<\infty$. At time zero, $$\label{eq:pre-insertion}
 \begin{split}
 \widetilde F_R^\varepsilon(0,Z_R)
 &=F_R^\varepsilon(0,Z_R)\\
 &=f_0^{\otimes R}(Z_R)\mathbf1_{{\cal D}_R^\varepsilon}(Z_R)
       \mathbb E\prod_{i\in R}\mathbf1_{\{N(B_i)=0\}},
 \qquad B_i=\overline B(x_i,\varepsilon).
 \end{split}$$ The expectation is over an independent cloud with the original law, and $N(B)$ counts its positions in $B$. Consequently $0\leq F_R^\varepsilon(0)\leq f_0^{\otimes R}$.*

<a id="eq:pre-initialcenter"></a>
<a id="eq:pre-centersmall"></a>
<a id="eq:pre-mass"></a>
*For $Z_R\in{\cal D}_R^\varepsilon$, define $$\label{eq:pre-initialcenter}
 E_R^\varepsilon(0,Z_R)=
 \sum_{S\subset R}(-1)^{|R\setminus S|}
      f_0^{\otimes(R\setminus S)}(Z_{R\setminus S})
      \widetilde F_S^\varepsilon(0,Z_S).$$ There are $c_0>0,C_0<\infty$, independent of $R,\varepsilon$, such that for sufficiently small $\varepsilon$, $$\label{eq:pre-centersmall}
 |E_R^\varepsilon(0,Z_R)|
       \leq(C_0\varepsilon^{c_0})^{|R|}f_0^{\otimes R}(Z_R).$$ One may take $c_0=1/125$. For fixed $r$, $$\label{eq:pre-mass}
 0\leq1-\int F_R^\varepsilon(0,Z_R)\,dZ_R
       \leq Cr\varepsilon+Cr^2\varepsilon^3,
 \qquad \int F_R^\varepsilon(t)=\int F_R^\varepsilon(0).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pre-initial"><summary>Proof of stmt-pre-initial</summary>

*Proof.* Insert $r$ particles into <a href="#pre:factorial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:factorial">[pre:factorial]</a> at time zero. Their mutual exclusions give $\mathbf1_{{\cal D}_R^\varepsilon}$. The remaining partition sum is $\mathcal Z_\varepsilon$ times the probability that the original cloud misses $\bigcup_iB_i$. This proves <a href="#eq:pre-insertion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre-insertion">[eq:pre-insertion]</a>. Initial exclusion already gives external isolation.

On $\mathcal D_R^\varepsilon$ every subset is separated, so substitution into <a href="#eq:pre-initialcenter" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre-initialcenter">[eq:pre-initialcenter]</a> gives the exact identity <a id="eq:pre-occupancy"></a>$$\label{eq:pre-occupancy}
 E_R^\varepsilon(0,Z_R)
 =(-1)^{|R|}f_0^{\otimes R}(Z_R)
       \mathbb P\{N(B_i)\geq1\text{ for every }i\in R\}.$$ Choose a maximal subcollection of centers with mutual distance greater than $2\varepsilon$. Its balls $B_i$ are disjoint up to null boundaries. A ball of radius $2\varepsilon$ contains at most $125$ original centers: their disjoint balls of radius $\varepsilon/2$ fit inside a ball of radius $5\varepsilon/2$. Hence the chosen subcollection has size $m\geq |R|/125$. Disjointness ensures that occupants are distinct, and the factorial upper bound gives $$\begin{aligned}
 \mathbb P\{N(B_i)\geq1\text{ for every chosen }i\}
 &\leq\mathbb E\prod_{\text{chosen }i}N(B_i)\\
 &=\mu^m\int_{\prod_i(B_i\times\mathbb R^3)}
                          F_m^\varepsilon(0)\,dZ_m\\
 &\leq\bigl(\mu\|\rho_0\|_\infty|B(0,\varepsilon)|\bigr)^m
 \leq(C\varepsilon)^m.
\end{aligned}$$ For $C\varepsilon\leq1$ this proves <a href="#eq:pre-centersmall" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre-centersmall">[eq:pre-centersmall]</a>.

The loss from mutual exclusion of $r$ independent inserted positions is at most $Cr^2\varepsilon^3$, by a union bound and bounded $\rho_0$. For each inserted position, $$\mathbb P\{N(B_i)\geq1\}\leq\mathbb E N(B_i)
 =\mu\int_{B_i\times\mathbb R^3}F_1^\varepsilon(0,z)\,dz
 \leq C\varepsilon.$$ A second union bound proves the first estimate in <a href="#eq:pre-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre-mass">[eq:pre-mass]</a>. The integral of <a href="#pre:factorial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:factorial">[pre:factorial]</a> is $\mu^{-r}\mathbb E(N)_r$, where $(N)_r=N(N-1)\cdots(N-r+1)$. It is finite by the initial factorial bound and conserved since $N$ is conserved. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Scattering flux and the collision operator

<a id="eq:pre-fluxdisk"></a>
Use microscopic relative coordinates in this subsection. For $g\ne0$ and $b\in g^\perp$, $|b|<1$, the incoming free trajectory meets the interaction sphere at $$n=b-\sqrt{1-|b|^2}\,\frac g{|g|},\qquad n\cdot g<0.$$ Time translation identifies the asymptotic prescription $q(\tau)-(b+\tau g)\to0$, $q'(\tau)\to g$ as $\tau\to-\infty$, with $q(0)=n$, $q'(0)=g$ for $q''=-2\nabla\Phi(q)$. Let $\tau_*(n,g)\in(0,\infty]$ be its first subsequent exit time from the open unit ball. Projection onto the impact disk has Jacobian $$\label{eq:pre-fluxdisk}
 |n\cdot g|\,d\sigma(n)=|g|\,d^2b.$$

<a id="stmt-pre-duration"></a>
<div id="pre:duration" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:pre-duration"></a>
**Lemma 5** (Residence-time flux). *For every $M<\infty$ there is $C_M<\infty$ such that $$\label{eq:pre-duration}
 \int_{\substack{|g|\leq M\\n\in S^2,\ n\cdot g<0}}
 \tau_*(n,g)|n\cdot g|\,d\sigma(n)\,dg\leq C_M.$$ The incoming flux of $\{\tau_*>D,\ |g|\leq M\}$ is at most $C_M/D$. In particular $\tau_*<\infty$ almost everywhere in incoming flux. The entrance-to-exit scattering map preserves flux.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pre-duration"><summary>Proof of stmt-pre-duration</summary>

*Proof.* Relative energy is $$\tfrac14|q'|^2+\phi(|q|)=\tfrac14|g|^2.$$ Since $\phi\geq-2B$, we have $|q'|^2\leq M^2+8B$ throughout the encounter. Consider $$(n,g,s)\longmapsto(q(s),q'(s)),
 \quad n\cdot g<0,\quad |g|\leq M,\quad0<s<\tau_*(n,g).$$ Its volume element is $|n\cdot g|\,d\sigma(n)\,dg\,ds$. At $s=0$, append the velocity vector field to five tangent vectors of the entrance section. Its normal component in position space is $n\cdot g$. Phase-volume preservation propagates that determinant.

The map is injective. Two representations of an interior state lie on the same orbit by uniqueness. A preceding outward crossing cannot be followed by an inward crossing: outside the ball the path is a straight line with increasing distance from the origin. If the orbit repeated an interior state it would be periodic, so it could not have a past entrance from outside. Hence there is at most one entrance and elapsed time.

Apply change of variables on compact pieces of the entrance section and bounded elapsed-time intervals, then exhaust the domain. The image lies in $B(0,1)\times B(0,\sqrt{M^2+8B})$. Its finite volume proves <a href="#eq:pre-duration" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre-duration">[eq:pre-duration]</a>. Markov’s inequality gives the tail and excludes infinite duration outside a null set. The same flow-box determinant on the transverse exit section proves flux preservation. Tangential section data carry zero flux. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-auto-definition-2"></a>
<div class="definition">

<a id="pre:collision"></a>
**Definition 6** (The scattering collision operator). Write $c=(v+v_*)/2$, $g=v-v_*$, and, for almost every incoming datum, let $g_{\rm out}$ be the velocity after exit. Set $$v'=c+\tfrac12g_{\rm out},\qquad
 v_*'=c-\tfrac12g_{\rm out}.$$ For $\psi\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^3)$, define $Q_\Phi$ at each $x$ by $$\label{pre:collision}
 \begin{split}
 \int Q_\Phi(f,f)(x,v)\psi(v)\,dv
 =\frac12\int_{\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3}
 & f(x,v)f(x,v_*)|v-v_*|\\
 {}\times\int_{\substack{b\in(v-v_*)^\perp\\|b|<1}}
 &[\,\psi(v')+\psi(v_*')-\psi(v)-\psi(v_*)\,]\,
                         d^2b\,dv\,dv_* .
 \end{split}$$ Here $d^2b$ is Euclidean area, and the $g=0$ contribution is zero. Null exceptional scattering data are ignored.

</div>

<a id="stmt-pre-operator"></a>
<div id="pre:operator" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:pre-Qstrong"></a>
**Lemma 7** (Reversibility and normalization). *The measure $d\Lambda=|v-v_*|\,dv\,dv_*\,d^2b$ is preserved by exchanging incoming and outgoing velocity pairs with the corresponding entrance parameter. The weak definition <a href="#pre:collision" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:collision">[pre:collision]</a> equals $$\label{eq:pre-Qstrong}
 Q_\Phi(f,f)(x,v)=
 \int_{\mathbb R^3}|v-v_*|
 \int_{\substack{b\in(v-v_*)^\perp\\|b|<1}}
 [\,f(x,v')f(x,v_*')-f(x,v)f(x,v_*)\,]\,d^2b\,dv_* .$$ These identities hold whenever the integrals are absolutely convergent, in particular for Gaussian bounded functions. All scattering branches are included, and the impact disk has area $\pi$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pre-operator"><summary>Proof of stmt-pre-operator</summary>

*Proof.* Let $(n_+,g_{\rm out})$ be the exit state of the relative orbit with entrance $(n,g)$. Since $\Phi$ is even, $-q(\tau_*-s)$ has entrance $(-n_+,g_{\rm out})$ and exit $(-n,g)$. Thus $(n,g)\mapsto(-n_+,g_{\rm out})$ is an involution of the incoming section outside a null set. It preserves flux by Lemma <a href="#pre:duration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:duration">5</a> and the reflection $n_+\mapsto-n_+$. The center velocity is unchanged, and $(v,v_*)\leftrightarrow(c,g)$ has absolute determinant one. Equation <a href="#eq:pre-fluxdisk" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre-fluxdisk">[eq:pre-fluxdisk]</a> proves invariance of $d\Lambda$.

Particle exchange also preserves $d\Lambda$, so the two test-function terms in either pair of <a href="#pre:collision" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:collision">[pre:collision]</a> give equal integrals. This cancels the factor $1/2$. The scattering involution transfers the remaining outgoing test to the incoming variable, giving <a href="#eq:pre-Qstrong" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre-Qstrong">[eq:pre-Qstrong]</a>. Energy conservation gives $|v'|^2+|v_*'|^2=|v|^2+|v_*|^2$. The finite disk area and Gaussian integration justify absolute convergence. No scattering-angle density or choice of one branch is introduced. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Exceptional deflections and rotational parameters

<a id="stmt-pre-scattering"></a>
<div id="pre:scattering" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:pre-increments"></a>
**Proposition 8** (Scattering nondegeneracy). *Outside a set of zero incoming flux, an encounter either is a straight force-free passage or satisfies $$\label{eq:pre-increments}
 v'\ne v,\qquad v_*'\ne v .$$ For $M<\infty$ and $\eta>0$, the non-free relative incoming data $(n,g)$ with $|g|\leq M$ can be restricted to a rotation-invariant compact set, losing at most $\eta$ relative incoming flux, on which the duration is bounded and $|g|$, $|v'-v|$, and $|v_*'-v|$ have positive lower bounds. For fixed radial scattering parameters, each of these nonzero increments has a uniform spherical directional marginal under common rotation. The two increments need not be independent.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pre-scattering"><summary>Proof of stmt-pre-scattering</summary>

<a id="eq:pre-Sardflux"></a>
*Proof.* Put $G=|g|$, $\rho=|b|$, $J=G\rho$, and $E=G^2/4$. The cases $G=0$, $J=0$, and $\rho=1$ are null in incoming flux. For $J>0$, the effective radial potential is $$W_J(r)=\phi(r)+\frac{J^2}{4r^2}.$$ An incoming turning radius $y$ satisfies $E=W_J(y)$. A nonsimple turning point also requires $W_J'(y)=0$. At each fixed $J$, the critical values of the $C^2$ function $W_J$ on $(0,1)$ are Lebesgue null, by the one-dimensional Sard theorem on a countable compact exhaustion. The radial incoming flux is $$\label{eq:pre-Sardflux}
 G^3\rho\,dG\,d\rho=2J\,dE\,dJ.$$ Fubini excludes nonsimple turning energies up to zero flux. Lemma <a href="#pre:duration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:duration">5</a> has already excluded infinite duration.

At a simple incoming turning radius $y\in(0,1)$, $W_J'(y)<0$ and $E>W_J(r)$ for all $r>y$. Set $a=J^{-2}$ and $$A_y(r)=y^{-2}-r^{-2},\qquad
 C_y(r)=4(\phi(y)-\phi(r)),\qquad
 H_{y,a}(r)=A_y(r)+aC_y(r).$$ The half angular sweep is <a id="eq:pre-halfangle"></a>$$\label{eq:pre-halfangle}
 {\cal A}_y(a)=\int_y^\infty\frac{dr}{r^2\sqrt{H_{y,a}(r)}}.$$ Indeed $\dot r^2=J^2H_{y,a}(r)$ and the angular speed is $J/r^2$. For admissible $a$, one has $H_{y,a}(r)>0$ for $r>y$, $\partial_rH_{y,a}(y)>0$, and $\lim_{r\to\infty}H_{y,a}(r)>0$. The ratios $C_y/A_y$ and $H_{y,a}/A_y$ extend continuously to $[y,\infty]$. At $y$ use the ratios of first derivatives; at infinity use finite range. The second ratio is strictly positive on this compact interval, so it has a positive minimum, while $C_y/H_{y,a}$ is bounded. These properties also show that the admissible set of $a$ is open.

For $a'$ near $a$, the binomial expansion in $$H_{y,a'}^{-1/2}
 =H_{y,a}^{-1/2}
       \left(1+(a'-a)\frac{C_y}{H_{y,a}}\right)^{-1/2}$$ converges uniformly after multiplication by the integrable majorant $r^{-2}H_{y,a}^{-1/2}$. Thus $\mathcal A_y$ is real analytic in the parameter $a$, although $\phi$ is only $C^2$. Twice differentiating gives <a id="eq:pre-convex"></a>$$\label{eq:pre-convex}
 {\cal A}_y''(a)=\frac34
       \int_y^\infty\frac{C_y(r)^2\,dr}{r^2H_{y,a}(r)^{5/2}}.$$ Near $y$ the integrand is $O((r-y)^{-1/2})$, since $C_y(r)=O(r-y)$ and $H_{y,a}$ has a simple root. It is also integrable at infinity. It is strictly positive unless $\phi(r)=\phi(y)$ for every $r\geq y$. Finite range makes this constant zero; the encountered orbit is then a straight force-free passage.

For a non-free encounter $\mathcal A_y$ is strictly convex. The deflection angle is $\pi-2\mathcal A_y$ modulo $2\pi$. Therefore $g_{\rm out}=g$ or $g_{\rm out}=-g$ requires $\mathcal A_y\in(\pi/2)\mathbb Z$. Each such level has at most two preimages on an admissible interval. There are countably many levels and countably many open intervals. On a simple turning branch, $(y,J)\mapsto(E,J)$, $E=W_J(y)$, is a local $C^1$ diffeomorphism. A countable cover by these charts, Fubini in $(y,a)$, and <a href="#eq:pre-Sardflux" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre-Sardflux">[eq:pre-Sardflux]</a> prove flux nullness. These exceptional equalities are exactly the two failures of <a href="#eq:pre-increments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre-increments">[eq:pre-increments]</a>.

For the compact-set assertion, exhaust the simple-scattering non-free radial parameter domain by compact subsets avoiding $G=0$, $J=0$, $\rho=1$, and the zero-increment levels. This covers all non-free data outside a null set. On each such compact subset, continuous dependence for the $C^1$ ODE flow and transversality of the finite exit give a continuous exit time and scattering map. Thus durations are bounded and all the listed nonzero quantities have positive minima. The relative flux measure on $G\leq M$ is finite, so an exhaustion member loses at most $\eta$ flux. Lifting with all rotations makes the set rotation-invariant.

Finally, write $(g,b)=(GRe_1,\rho Re_2)$, $R\in SO(3)$. At fixed $(G,\rho)$ the orientation factor in incoming flux is a constant multiple of Haar measure on $SO(3)$. Central-force rotation equivariance maps each nonzero increment to $R$ times a fixed vector. Its direction has uniform measure on $S^2$. This is a marginal statement and leaves the joint pair correlated. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### A fixed spatial Gaussian envelope on the kinetic interval

Use the norm $\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\alpha}$ defined in <a href="#intro:norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:norm">[intro:norm]</a>, and put $$\|h\|_{\infty,\alpha}
       =\sup_{x,v}e^{\alpha|v|^2}|h(x,v)|.$$ For vector-valued functions use Euclidean norm. The following proof adapts the weighted transport estimate in (Deng, Hani, and Ma 2025, Proposition A.1) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[DHM25](#ref-DHM25)]. Only finite impact area and kinetic energy conservation are needed, so the argument applies to $Q_\Phi$.

<a id="stmt-pre-envelopes"></a>
<div id="pre:envelopes" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:pre-envelope"></a>
**Proposition 9** (Known-solution envelopes). *Suppose $f$ is the classical nonnegative solution in the statement, and $$A=\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|f(t)\|_{\infty,2\beta}<\infty,\qquad
 B_0=\|f_0\|_{\mathrm{Bol},2\beta}
               +\|\nabla_xf_0\|_{\mathrm{Bol},2\beta}<\infty.$$ Then $$\label{eq:pre-envelope}
 \sup_{0\leq t\leq T}
 \left(\|f(t)\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\beta}
       +\|\nabla_xf(t)\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\beta}\right)
       \leq C(A,B_0,\beta,T)<\infty.$$ Fix, once and for all, <a id="eq:pre-beta0"></a>$$\label{eq:pre-beta0}
 \beta_0=\beta/2.$$ Thus all independent inputs $f(t)$ and their spatial Lipschitz envelopes have a common bound with the fixed margin $\beta-\beta_0$. Constants in <a href="#eq:pre-envelope" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre-envelope">[eq:pre-envelope]</a> are independent of the subsequent time-layer subdivision.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pre-envelopes"><summary>Proof of stmt-pre-envelopes</summary>

<a id="eq:pre-positiveB"></a>
*Proof.* Set $$\gamma(t)=\frac{3\beta}{2}-\frac{\beta t}{2T},\qquad
 c=\frac{\beta}{2T},\qquad M(v)=e^{-2\beta|v|^2},$$ and $(S(r)h)(x,v)=h(x-rv,v)$. Notice $\beta\leq\gamma(t)\leq3\beta/2$. Define a positive linear operator for $h\geq0$ by $$\label{eq:pre-positiveB}
 \begin{split}
 ({\cal B}h)(x,v)=\int_{\mathbb R^3}|v-v_*|\int_{|b|<1}
 \bigl[&M(v_*')h(x,v')+M(v')h(x,v_*')\\
       &+M(v_*)h(x,v)+M(v)h(x,v_*)\bigr]\,d^2b\,dv_* .
 \end{split}$$ The disk is in $(v-v_*)^\perp$.

<a id="eq:pre-VolterraBol"></a>
<a id="eq:pre-Volterrainfty"></a>
We first prove that for $0\leq s<t\leq T$, $r=t-s$, $$\begin{aligned}
 \|S(r){\cal B}h\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\gamma(t)}
       &\leq Cr^{-1/2}\|h\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\gamma(s)},
                    \label{eq:pre-VolterraBol}\\
 \|S(r){\cal B}h\|_{\infty,\gamma(t)}
       &\leq Cr^{-1/2}\|h\|_{\infty,\gamma(s)}.
                    \label{eq:pre-Volterrainfty}
\end{aligned}$$ Let $H_s(y)=\sup_u e^{\gamma(s)|u|^2}h(y,u)$. For each product in <a href="#eq:pre-positiveB" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre-positiveB">[eq:pre-positiveB]</a>, energy conservation, or its identity version for a loss product, gives $$M(u_*)h(y,u)
       \leq H_s(y)e^{-\gamma(s)(|v|^2+|v_*|^2)}.$$ After multiplication by $e^{\gamma(t)|v|^2}$ this is at most $$H_s(y)e^{-\beta|v_*|^2}
                    e^{-cr(|v|^2+|v_*|^2)}.$$ Since $$|v-v_*|e^{-cr(|v|^2+|v_*|^2)/2}\leq C_c r^{-1/2},$$ the disk area $\pi$ and the Gaussian integral in $v_*$ imply <a id="eq:pre-spatialmajorant"></a>$$\label{eq:pre-spatialmajorant}
 e^{\gamma(t)|v|^2}(S(r){\cal B}h)(x,v)
       \leq Cr^{-1/2}e^{-cr|v|^2/2}H_s(x-rv).$$ This proves <a href="#eq:pre-Volterrainfty" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre-Volterrainfty">[eq:pre-Volterrainfty]</a>.

<a id="eq:pre-freebound"></a>
For the summed spatial estimate put $a_k=\sup_{|y-k|\leq1}H_s(y)$. If $m\leq r|v|<m+1$ and $|x-k|\leq1$, then $|x-rv-k|\leq m+2$, whence $$H_s(x-rv)\leq
       \sum_{\substack{\ell\in\mathbb Z^3\\|\ell-k|\leq m+3}}a_\ell.$$ Indeed lattice-centered unit balls cover $\mathbb R^3$. For $r\leq T$, the damping factor on that shell is at most $e^{-cm^2/(2T)}$. Bound the supremum over $v$ by summation over $m\geq0$ and then sum over $k$. Each $a_\ell$ occurs at most $C(m+4)^3$ times. Convergence of $\sum_{m\geq0}e^{-cm^2/(2T)}(m+4)^3$ proves <a href="#eq:pre-VolterraBol" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre-VolterraBol">[eq:pre-VolterraBol]</a>. The same shell argument, now using $$e^{\gamma(t)|v|^2}|S(t)h_0(x,v)|
 \leq e^{-ct|v|^2}
       \sup_u e^{\gamma(0)|u|^2}|h_0(x-tv,u)|,$$ gives $$\label{eq:pre-freebound}
 \sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|S(t)h_0\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\gamma(t)}
       \leq C\|h_0\|_{\mathrm{Bol},\gamma(0)}.$$ At $t=0$ this is immediate.

<a id="eq:pre-remaindergamma"></a>
Define $$({\cal V}h)(t)=A\int_0^t S(t-s){\cal B}h(s)\,ds.$$ The characteristic equation, $f\geq0$, and $f\leq AM$ imply <a id="eq:pre-linearcomparison"></a>$$\label{eq:pre-linearcomparison}
 0\leq f(t)\leq S(t)f_0+({\cal V}f)(t).$$ Its $n$-fold iteration is the finite inequality $$f\leq\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}{\cal V}^j(S(\cdot)f_0)+{\cal V}^n f.$$ No finiteness of the unknown summed spatial norm has been assumed.
